# Roles Tested

Real-host `krikri-playbook`-vs-`ansible-playbook` correctness rounds run so
far, and each role's **current** status (not what was fixed to get there —
see `KNOWN_MISSING.md` and `git log` for that history). Check this before
picking a shortlist for the next round, to avoid re-discovering Galaxy-404s
or already-clean roles as if they were new.

Every newly tested role gets its own row (not bundled into a shared
"role / role / role" line) with cold/warm timings for both engines where
the run produced a real timing (rc=0 or a genuine both-fail past setup) -
see `CLAUDE.md`'s benchmark-round workflow. Rows predating that convention
may be bundled and/or missing timings; not backfilled.

## Benchmark: 10 random verified-clean roles, fresh host pair each

Measured against real `ansible-playbook` (`ansible-core` 2.19.4): 10 real
Galaxy roles drawn at random from the verified-clean list below, each on
its own **fresh** Atlantic.net `G3.2GB` Ubuntu-22.04 host pair (one host
per engine, never reused, destroyed immediately after), cold (first touch)
and warm (idempotent re-run) on both engines, `--forks 1` on both sides.
krikri-playbook was built `--release` and stripped, and ran with
`--persistent-daemon --no-batching` (one long-lived
`ssh ... -- <plugin binary> --daemon` connection per host instead of a
fresh `ssh`+`bash`+exec per task; batching was disabled during measurement
because, at the time, it routed around that path). **That caveat is now
historical** - as of 0.9.635 batched groups go over the daemon too, so the
two features compose and a re-run of this table would no longer need
`--no-batching`. The numbers below predate that and are not re-measured
here. `PLAY RECAP` parity with real Ansible (`ok=`/`changed=`/`failed=`/
`skipped=`) was checked per role, cold AND warm, and matched exactly on
all 10:

| Role (author) | Python cold | Crystal cold | Cold speedup | Python warm | Crystal warm | Warm speedup |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `robertdebock.remi` | 4.39s | 3.55s | 1.2x | 2.82s | **0.72s** | **3.9x** |
| `geerlingguy.helm` | 27.28s | **9.74s** | **2.8x** | 5.81s | **1.66s** | **3.5x** |
| `geerlingguy.clamav` | 74.15s | **52.19s** | 1.4x | 29.53s | **2.49s** | **11.9x** |
| `geerlingguy.node_exporter` | 54.64s | **8.16s** | **6.7x** | 19.56s | **2.32s** | **8.4x** |
| `robertdebock.types` | 5.34s | 3.34s | 1.6x | 3.15s | **0.65s** | **4.8x** |
| `robertdebock.docker_ce` | 81.13s | **50.45s** | 1.6x | 15.56s | **2.54s** | **6.1x** |
| `robertdebock.digitalocean_agent` | 37.22s | **23.29s** | 1.6x | 17.60s | **2.37s** | **7.4x** |
| `geerlingguy.adminer` | 15.79s | **9.50s** | 1.7x | 9.00s | **2.01s** | **4.5x** |
| `robertdebock.upgrade` | 9.24s | **4.84s** | 1.9x | 6.90s | **1.59s** | **4.3x** |
| `robertdebock.fail2ban` | 39.62s | **21.63s** | 1.8x | 22.33s | **2.65s** | **8.4x** |

Cold runs are dominated by real apt/download time (both engines wait on
the same mirrors), so the trustworthy signal is the warm column: crystal
warm runs are **3.5x-11.9x faster (mean ~6.3x)**, cold runs 1.2x-6.7x
faster (mean ~2.2x). Real Ansible pays a fresh Python-interpreter-and-
module cost per task on every run regardless of whether anything changes;
krikri-playbook's compiled-binary-plus-persistent-connection model is why
its warm numbers drop so far below its own cold.

| buluma.ara_api | ✅ Fixed (round 190, 0.9.625, Ubuntu 22.04). THREE stacked engine bugs, all found via this one role: (1) it ships defaults/main.yaml (not .yml) and the loader only checked main.yml, so EVERY default was undefined; (2) `command: ara-manage` + `environment: PATH: <venv>/bin` failed - Process.new(env:) does not affect the execvp lookup; (3) the nested task-vars dict reconciled_configuration kept its bare-mustache values as unevaluated strings and set_fact stringified the whole container as Python-repr text (`DATABASE_CONN_MAX_AGE: "0"`, `DEBUG: "False"`), crashing Django's settings load. All three fixed (find_main_file, command PATH resolution, recursive type-preserving render_task_vars + set_fact native containers); live-verified on a fresh single host: settings.yaml now byte-shape-identical to real ansible's. py 39.5s/13.1s cold/warm. |
| buluma.multi | ✅ Fixed (round 190, 0.9.625, Ubuntu 22.04). `lookup('config', 'COLOR_OK', 'COLOR_CHANGED', 'COLOR_SKIP', wantlist=True)` was unimplemented -> `'item' is undefined` on the multi-arg config loop. Both evaluators now implement lookup('config') with ansible-core 2.19 defaults + ANSIBLE_<NAME> env honouring. |
| mrlesmithjr.ansible_es_apm_server | ✅ Fixed (round 190, 0.9.625, Ubuntu 22.04). `apt_key: file: /tmp/GPG-KEY-elasticsearch` was unimplemented ("Missing required parameter: url or data") - file: is now supported (target-side path, matching real Ansible's semantics). |
| buluma.handbrake | ✅ Fixed (round 190, 0.9.625, Ubuntu 22.04). Whole role SILENTLY ran as zero tasks (crystal rc=0 ok=0, "no tasks defined") because its tasks/main.yaml was invisible to the main.yml-only loader. Same find_main_file fix as ara_api. |

Env/role-side, not fixed. |
| weareinteractive.ntp | ✅ Fixed and verified (rounds 177/179, 0.9.589). Cold run initially diverged (`rc=2` real ansible vs `rc=4` krikri-playbook) because krikri-playbook's SSH transport hit an Atlantic.net IP reused from an earlier role in the same batch, with a DIFFERENT host key still in `known_hosts` - it hardcoded `StrictHostKeyChecking=accept-new` (refuses a CHANGED key) regardless of `ANSIBLE_HOST_KEY_CHECKING`, while real ansible's `host_key_checking=False` uses `StrictHostKeyChecking=no` (accepts a changed key too, verified in ansible-core's own `ssh.py`). Fixed to honor `ANSIBLE_HOST_KEY_CHECKING`/`ANSIBLE_SSH_HOST_KEY_CHECKING`. |
| weareinteractive.sftp | ✅ Fixed (round 178, 0.9.590), not re-verified live. Its own `meta/main.yml` depends on `franklinkim.ssh`, a role no longer published anywhere - real ansible refuses the whole run immediately with `rc=1` ("the role ... was not found"); krikri-playbook fell through to its generic parser-error `rc=4`. Fixed with a dedicated `RoleNotFoundError` (also covers a play-level missing `roles:` entry, same rc=1); confirmed against a local repro matching this exact failure shape and message. |
| buluma.cve_2024_3094 | ✅ Fixed (round 189, 0.9.624): regex_search no-match returned the "undefined" sentinel string instead of None, so the list-form failed_when `(stdout | regex_search(...)) is not none` was TRUE for a no-match and failed a succeeding task (cr rc=2 vs py rc=0 cold+warm). Fixed in both evaluators (FilterEngine + Crinja) plus the is-none bridge in matches_type_test?; regression spec added; repro verified locally flipped to rc=0. Original find (round 189, 0.9.623, Ubuntu 22.04). The role's list-form `failed_when:` (`- (xz_version.stdout | ansible.builtin.regex_search("5\\.6\\.(0|1)")) is not none`) fails the task on krikri-playbook even when the command succeeded and the regex does NOT match - real ansible evaluates it false and the task is `ok`. Crystal rc=2 cold+warm vs real rc=0; repro: a 1-task playbook, py rc=0 / cr rc=2 locally. Same strict-evaluator family as the round-185 list-form `when:` bug but in the `failed_when:` path (filter chain over a registered result feeding `is not none`). Timing: real 16.06s/11.87s cold/warm vs crystal 4.17s/1.26s. |
| buluma.update | ✅ Clean behaviorally (round 189, Ubuntu 22.04). Real DOES show a real apt upgrade: 347.46s cold / 7.41s warm vs crystal 325.23s / 8.25s. Cosmetic recap accounting gap (crystal counts the implicit gather in ok= -> ok=7/6 cold, ok=2/1, skip alignment shifted one row). |
| buluma.virtualbox | ✅ Clean (round 189, Ubuntu 22.04). | mrlesmithjr.change-hostname | ✅ Fixed (round 189, 0.9.624): async:/poll:0 over SSH was refused ("async only supported for local connections"), so the fire-and-forget reboot never happened (cr rc=0 vs py rc=2). Remote async now mirrors real Ansible ~/.ansible_async: nohup-detached plugin on the target, status file polled over SSH. Original find (round 189, Ubuntu 22.04). The role reboots via `shell: shutdown -r now` + `async: 1`/`poll: 0`: real ansible fires the async reboot (host actually restarted), krikri-playbook refuses with "async: is only supported for local connections" (swallowed by the role's own ignore_errors) so its host never reboots -> rc=0 vs real's rc=2. Async-over-SSH fire-and-forget needs implementing (or documenting); role itself otherwise fine (hostname module set hostname correctly on both, verified via each engine's own change message). Real's post-reboot wait_for failed once on an interpreter-discovery flake (/usr/bin/python3.10 not found seconds after boot) - environmental, clean on warm rerun. Times: real 5.01s cold / 0.52s warm vs crystal 13.96s / 0.63s. |
| mrlesmithjr.timezone | ✅ Clean (round 189, Ubuntu 22.04). Identical outcomes: timezone set changed=1 cold, hardware-clock variant skipped, idempotent warm rc=0 changed=0 both. Real 2.25s/1.07s vs crystal 2.53s/0.33s. |
| mrlesmithjr.network-tweaks | ✅ Fixed (round 189, 0.9.624): YAML folded scalars embed real newlines when continuation lines are more-indented, and ConditionalEvaluator mis-split on them, silently evaluating the compound when: FALSE for every loop item (zero sysctls applied vs 9 on real ansible). Fixed with a quote-aware whitespace normalizer; regression spec added; repro verified. Original find (round 189, 0.9.623, Ubuntu 22.04). A folded multi-line compound `when:` (`(network_tweaks_enable is defined and network_tweaks_enable) and (item.set is defined and item.set)`) over a with_items loop evaluates FALSE for every item on krikri-playbook - all 11 items skipped, zero sysctls applied - while real ansible runs the 9 set:true ones (changed) and correctly gates the rest. py rc=0 changed=1 vs cr rc=0 changed=0. Repro saved (round189/repro/nt.yml): same playbook, real attempts the write, crystal skips. Likely the newline/join or nested-paren `is defined and var` handling in ConditionalEvaluator silently evaluating to false instead of erroring. Times: real 6.71s/5.12s vs crystal 2.79s/0.25s (fast because it did nothing). |

| geerlingguy.selenium | ✅ Fixed and verified (round 176, 0.9.588). Cold run initially diverged: real ansible correctly failed "Install Chrome (if configured, RedHat)" with a GPG-signature error (`google-chrome-stable` RPM installed straight from a URL, no key imported) while krikri-playbook's `yum:`/`dnf:`/`package:` plugins installed it anyway - dnf's own actual default for `localpkg_gpgcheck` is OFF (verified against real ansible-core's own `dnf.py`: `conf.localpkg_gpgcheck = not disable_gpg_check`, which the module always sets explicitly regardless of dnf.conf). Fixed by always passing `--setopt=localpkg_gpgcheck=1` (or `--nogpgcheck` when `disable_gpg_check: true`) in `yum.cr`/`dnf.cr`/`package.cr`. Re-verified live on a fresh Rocky 9.6 pair - both engines now fail identically with the same GPG error, cold and warm. |

| buluma.selinux | ✅ Regression root-caused and fixed (round 65203/65401 found it, live-reverified on a fresh Kata Rocky VM, 0.9.830): `plugins/selinux.cr` treated a missing `/etc/selinux/config` as a deliberate no-op ("SELinux not compiled in") - checked against `ansible.posix.selinux`'s own module source, real Ansible has no such case and always `fail_json`s ("Unable to find file /etc/selinux/config") regardless of distro. Fixed to match exactly. No spec (hardcodes the real system path); verified live pre/post-fix. Also fixed as a side effect: real Ansible's bare `...ignoring` line after ANY ignored task failure (not just a when:-raise) was never printed at all before - now is, in `ResultDisplay.display_result` generally. |

Fixed to match that exact condition. Live-reverified: `ok=7 changed=0 skipped=5` matching real ansible-playbook's own idempotent recap once the system was actually up to date (task-name-prefix and unimplemented-zypper/pacman/apk-module skip-line differences are cosmetic/pre-existing, not new). |
| *(not a Galaxy role - real module implementation)* `ansible.builtin.reboot` | ✅ Implemented (round 137, 0.9.444). Entirely unimplemented before (silently dropped at parse time) - found via `robertdebock.common`'s and `.update`'s own reboot-on-change handlers, both common real-world idioms. Architecturally can't be a normal remotely-uploaded plugin binary (the process would die with the machine mid-reboot) - implemented as a controller-side special case (`TaskExecutor#execute_reboot`): issues the reboot command tolerating the connection dying mid-command, then polls a trivial remote command until the host comes back or `reboot_timeout` is exceeded. Excluded from SSH batching and the eager per-play plugin pre-upload pass (same treatment `delegate_to:`/`connection:` already get, for the same "no single shared connection survives this" reason). Live-verified end-to-end: a real reboot task against a live Atlantic.net host completed, the host genuinely rebooted (`uptime` showed 0 min after), and the playbook correctly waited, reconnected, and continued. `local` connection is intentionally refused (would reboot the controller's own machine). |

| robertdebock.backup | ✅ Clean (round 136, 0.9.440-0.9.441). 3 real bugs found and fixed: `community.general.archive` crashed the WHOLE archive build on a symlink-to-directory member (Debian's own default `/var/spool/mail -> ../mail`, this role's own default backup target) - fixed with a real tar SYMLINK entry (full parity) and a documented zip-format-capability skip (Crystal's stdlib Zip writer has no Unix-attrs API); task-level `connection:` (distinct from `delegate_to:` - changes HOW a task's module runs, not WHICH host's vars apply) was never parsed at all, so the role's own "Create backup_directory" (`connection: local`, writes to the controller's filesystem) silently ran against the real remote target instead - fixed by parsing it and threading it into `ansible_connection` for that task's own dispatch, plus excluding it from SSH batching (same treatment `delegate_to:` already gets). Needed `ansible_python_interpreter=/usr/bin/python3` in the inventory (real ansible-playbook's own `connection: local` sub-task otherwise inherits the REMOTE host's auto-discovered python3.10 path, which this sandbox's controller doesn't have - an environment mismatch, not a role or engine bug). Live-reverified: byte-identical `ok=12 changed=2 skipped=5` cold, `ok=12 changed=0 skipped=5` idempotent warm rerun on both, `backups/<hostname>/` correctly created on the controller by both engines. |
| robertdebock.restore | ✅ Clean (round 136, no version bump - zero krikri-playbook bugs found). Byte-identical `ok=8 changed=1 skipped=10` cold, `ok=8 changed=0 skipped=10` idempotent warm rerun on both. |

| robertdebock.dns | ✅ Clean (round 135, 0.9.433-0.9.435). 3 real bugs found and fixed, none role-specific - all three were general engine gaps this role happened to be the first to exercise: `--limit` was parsed but never actually applied anywhere, silently running every play against the WHOLE inventory regardless (fixed in `krikri-playbook.cr`, plus a regression spec - previously zero coverage for the flag); the round 132 "skip bash -c" local-exec optimization's shell-metacharacter detection missed a leading `NAME=value` env-assignment prefix (`DEBIAN_FRONTEND=noninteractive apt-get install ...`, apt.cr/package.cr's own real command), breaking every local-connection apt install; and a role-relative `copy: src:` (nested inside a `block:`, over a real SSH connection - previously untested combination) never got staged to the remote host at all when the resolved path was relative (the common case), because the "does this need staging" gate required an absolute path. Live-reverified: `ok=24 changed=9 skipped=7` cold and `ok=22 changed=0 skipped=7` idempotent warm rerun, byte-identical to real ansible-playbook on both, `named.conf.local` md5-identical, bind9 active on both. |

| robertdebock.openssl | ✅ Clean (round 135, 0.9.436), and its `community.crypto` tasks now actually run (0.9.608): re-verified locally against real ansible-core 2.19.4 with `connection: local` (the role's own root-only "Install requirements" apt task removed - it fails identically on both engines as a non-root user). Previously every one of those tasks was an unavailable module (`rc=4`). 1 real bug found and fixed: a when:-gated `include_tasks:` (the role's own looped `include_tasks: create.yml`, `loop: "{{ openssl_items }}"` undefined by default, gated by `when: openssl_items is defined`) incremented recap `ok` unconditionally BEFORE its own when: check ran, then `skipped` again once that check failed - double-counted as both for the same single skipped task, inflating `ok=` by one. Fixed in `execute_include_tasks`/`run_include_tasks_once` (the multi-host batched path already partitioned run/skip hosts correctly and didn't have this bug). | robertdebock.swap | ✅ Clean (round 135, 0.9.438). 2 real bugs found and fixed: `community.general.filesystem` was entirely unimplemented (new `plugins/filesystem.cr` - state present/absent, blkid-based existing-fs detection, force/opts, most Linux fstypes; `resizefs:`/`uuid:` not implemented) - the role's own "Make swap file system" (`fstype: swap` on a freshly dd'd swap file) silently dropped; and a `block:`'s own `notify:` (the role's own "Manage swap files." block, wrapping 4 nested tasks none of which have their own notify:, itself notifying "Run swapon") was silently dropped at TWO levels - `parse_block_task` never parsed the `notify:` key at all, and `execute_block`/`execute_block_multi` never checked it even when present, only ever forwarding an individual nested task's own notify:. Both fixed. Live-reverified: byte-identical `ok=11 changed=5 skipped=2` cold, `ok=10 changed=0 skipped=2` idempotent warm rerun on both - the role's own handler (`swapon` with no arguments, no `-a` flag) doesn't actually activate the swap file on EITHER engine (confirmed identically broken on real ansible-playbook too - `swapon --show` empty on both hosts after a full run), a real external role bug, not an engine issue. |
| robertdebock.sudo_pair | ✅ Clean (round 135, 0.9.439). 1 real bug found and fixed: `zip_changed?`'s idempotency check compared a symlink zip member's DEREFERENCED content (`md5sum < dest_path`, shell redirection always follows symlinks) against the archive's own raw member content (a zip symlink entry's content IS the short target-path string, not the target's content) - permanently mismatched even though extraction was byte-correct, so the role's own "Download and unpack sudo_pair" (square/sudo_pair's GitHub release zip symlinks a shared LICENSE/README into several sub-crate dirs) was re-extracted on every single rerun. Fixed by comparing `readlink`'s own output instead of dereferencing. Needed `unzip`, a full `rustup`-installed toolchain (`cargo`/`rustc` symlinked into `/usr/local/bin`, the role's own PATH override), and `gcc` installed separately - the role's own `sudo_pair_requirements` only lists `libclang-dev`, missing its own real build dependencies (external role gap, reproduces identically on real ansible-playbook too). Live-reverified: byte-identical `ok=8 changed=4 skipped=4` cold, `ok=8 changed=0 skipped=4` idempotent warm rerun on both after the fix. |
| robertdebock.mitogen | ✅ Clean (round 133, 0.9.427). 1 real bug found and fixed: `ansible_facts['python']` was a flat string (executable path) stored under two invented keys (`ansible_python`/`ansible_python_version`) that don't exist under those names in real Ansible - real Ansible's own `PythonFactCollector` exposes a single nested dict (`version.major`/`.minor`/`.micro`, `version_info`, `executable`, `has_sslcontext`, `type`). The role's own `python{{ ansible_facts['python'].version.major }}` command construction resolved to the literal string `pythonundefined` instead of `python3`, crashing the very first real task. Rewrote `facts.cr`'s `gather_python_facts` to introspect the interpreter itself (`python3 -c '...json.dumps(...)'`) and build the correct nested structure, matching real Ansible's schema exactly. Live-reverified on a fresh Atlantic.net pair: `ok=7 changed=3 failed=0` identical cold, fully idempotent warm rerun on both (recap `skipped` vs `ok` categorization for the `unarchive: creates:` short-circuit differs cosmetically, a separate pre-existing gap, not new). |

| robertdebock.vagrant | ✅ Clean (round 131, 0.9.426). 1 real bug found and fixed: `pip.cr`'s `name:` never handled a `{{ }}`-templated list variable rendering to its own bracketed text form (same "Python-repr-list JSON" class as apt.cr/package.cr/dnf.cr/find.cr) - the role's own `name: "{{ vagrant_pip_packages }}"` (an empty list on Debian) fed pip the literal string `"[]"`, crashing with `ERROR: Invalid requirement: '[]'` instead of real Ansible's clean `if name:` falsy-empty-list no-op. Fixed with a `normalize_name` helper (empty list -> nil/no-op, single-element list -> unwrap, matching real `pip.py`'s `required_one_of`/truthiness semantics exactly); multi-package `name:` lists remain a separate, pre-existing, documented single-package-only scope limit. Live-reverified: `ok=6 changed=0 failed=0` identical on both engines after the fix (skip-count differs only via the pre-existing `community.general.zypper` unimplemented-module gap, same class as `rpm_key`/`zypper_repository`/`seport`). |

| robertdebock.earlyoom | ✅ Clean (round 98, 0.9.412-0.9.413). 2 real bugs. `git.cr`'s `resolve_ref` used `rev-parse <ref>` which returns an ANNOTATED tag's own object SHA (not the commit it points to) for `version: v1.6` - never matched `current_commit`'s always-a-real-commit-SHA, so every rerun re-checked-out and reported changed:true, never converging; fixed by peeling through `^{commit}`, matching real Ansible's own approach. Also, `community.general.make` was entirely unimplemented - new `plugins/make.cr` added, ported exactly from real Ansible's own module source (idempotency via `make ... -q`). Found via "Make earlyoom"/"Install earlyoom" both notified by the same task - "Make earlyoom" was silently dropped at parse time, so the earlyoom binary never got built and the copy-based "Install earlyoom" handler failed. | robertdebock.tailscale | ✅ Clean (round 96, 0.9.410-0.9.411; 1 more real bug found and fixed round 134, 0.9.428). 3 real bugs found and fixed round 96: (1) a PARSE-TIME crash ("Can't convert Bool to a JSON object key") took down the entire playbook - the role's own vars/main.yml uses a dict keyed by a bare YAML boolean (a real supported Ansible/Jinja2 idiom), which RoleLoader.load_vars_file's JSON round-trip crashed on; fixed with a recursive key-stringifying converter. (2) a real Crystal 1.20.3 stdlib HTTP::Client bug silently truncated a chunked HTTPS response body for pkgs.tailscale.com (200 OK, no error, wrong byte count) - apt_key.cr's url: fetch now shells out to curl instead. (3) apt_key.cr's keyring: parameter was entirely unimplemented, silently breaking any role pairing apt_key: keyring: with a matching apt_repository:/deb822 signed-by= path - fixed by threading --keyring through every apt-key subcommand. Round 134: the upstream role has since gained a Debian-13/Ubuntu-24.04 `deb822` repo path as a sibling `block:` to the older `sources.list` path, both `register:`-ing the same name (`_deb_repository`) - on Ubuntu 22.04 the `deb822` block is the one skipped, but its nested task's `register:` was never reapplied (a task skipped only via its ENCLOSING block's `when:`, not its own, never went through `register_skip_result`), so `_deb_repository` kept the sources.list block's real `changed: true`, wrongly running the downstream "Update apt cache" task instead of skipping it like real Ansible. Fixed. Live-reverified: `tailscaled` active and functional, recap byte-identical to real Ansible (`ok=7 changed=3` cold, `changed=0` warm) on both engines; skip-count diff is the pre-existing `rpm_key`/`zypper_repository`/`zypper` unimplemented-module gap (RHEL/SUSE-only), not new. |

| robertdebock.cups | ⚠️ Real gap found, not fixed (round 68, no version bump). The pinned Galaxy release's own `template: validate: cupsd -t -c %s` (missing `-s /etc/cups/cups-files.conf`, already fixed upstream in newer releases) makes `cupsd -t`'s cups-files.conf lookup implicitly relative to the validated file's OWN directory. Real Ansible's `template:` renders its validate temp file under its own remote-tmp location (not `/etc/cups/`), so `cupsd -t` can't find `cups-files.conf` there and the task genuinely fails on real `ansible-playbook` too (confirmed deterministic, not a flake, via a second full run). krikri-playbook's own `template.cr` deliberately colocates its validate temp file with the destination directory (`/etc/cups/`, for a same-filesystem atomic rename) - `cupsd -t` finds `cups-files.conf` right there and validation (harmlessly) succeeds, so crystal completes the role cleanly where real Ansible fails. A genuine template-temp-file-location divergence, not a quick fix (relocating it to match real Ansible's remote-tmp risks breaking the atomic `File.rename` across filesystems for every OTHER `template:`/`validate:` use) - documented rather than same-session-patched, same precedent as round 52/62. `cups` service functionally healthy on the crystal host (`active`, HTTP 200 on :631) regardless. |

| robertdebock.httpd (+ ansible.posix, community.general) | ✅ Clean (round 51, 0.9.390). 1 real bug found and fixed: `RoleLoader#resolve_role_dir` never searched `~/.ansible/roles`/`ANSIBLE_ROLES_PATH` - the default install location for a plain (non-collection) `ansible-galaxy role install`, breaking any bare Galaxy-role reference from a cwd without a local `roles/` checkout ("Role not found"). Fixed with a `roles_paths` search list mirroring the existing `collections_paths` handling. Live-reverified on a fresh `G3.2GB` Atlantic.net pair: cold `ok=18 changed=4 failed=0 skipped=28` (real) vs `ok=18 changed=4 failed=0 skipped=25` (crystal) - skip-count gap is the pre-existing documented `seport`/`seboolean`/`apache2_module` cosmetic scope gap, not new. Both `apache2` `active`, HTTP 200, fully idempotent warm rerun (`changed=0` both). |
| geerlingguy.kubernetes (+ geerlingguy.containerd) | ✅ Clean (round 35, 0.9.382; first new role since round 33's nomad). Real 2-vCPU/4GB Atlantic.net pair (resized up from 1 vCPU mid-round - kubeadm's control-plane needs 2). 3 real engine bugs found and fixed, all surfaced by the role's own "Set the kubeadm join command globally." task (`loop: "{{ groups['all'] }}", delegate_to: "{{ item }}", delegate_facts: true`): the `groups` magic variable was entirely unpopulated (always "undefined"), a templated `delegate_to:` on a loop was resolved once before the loop bound `item` (crashed trying to SSH to a host literally named "undefined"), and `delegate_facts:` was entirely unimplemented (the delegated fact silently stayed on the executing host instead of the target). Also hit the same stale-`ansible_default_ipv4` Atlantic.net quirk documented for round 33's nomad (worked around by pinning `kubernetes_apiserver_advertise_address`, not an engine bug). Final: real Kubernetes control-plane node `Ready` with every system pod `Running` (etcd/apiserver/scheduler/controller-manager/coredns/kube-proxy/flannel) on both engines identically, idempotent warm rerun matching task-for-task. **Re-verified round 36 (same version, no new bugs)**: extended to a genuine 2-node cluster per engine (control-plane + worker, 4 hosts total, real `kubeadm join`) specifically to stress-test the round-35 `groups`/`delegate_to`/`delegate_facts` fixes under real cross-host orchestration (the single-node round never actually had a second host to delegate *to*). Also needed `kubernetes_join_command_extra_opts: "--ignore-preflight-errors=all"` pinned in the playbook - the role's own `node-setup.yml` never applies `kubernetes_ignore_preflight_errors` to `kubeadm join` (only to `init`), so a real preflight-fatal (`ip_forward` not yet 1, port 10250 transiently held by the un-joined kubelet) failed identically on real `ansible-playbook` too - a role gap, not an engine one. With that, both a real python-orchestrated and a real krikri-playbook-orchestrated 2-node cluster came up fully `Ready` (both nodes, all 10 system pods `Running`), idempotent warm rerun `changed=0` on both. See `KNOWN_MISSING.md`'s `0.9.382` entry. **Round 38 perf follow-up**: after round 37's `include_tasks:`/`block:` parallelism fix (`0.9.383`), a finer-grained `pexpect`-timed re-run on a fresh 2-node pair showed krikri-playbook now *faster* than real `ansible-playbook` on both cold (128.6s vs 196.7s) and warm (15.5s vs 45.1s) runs - not just closer, as round 37's own less-precise timing pass had estimated. No new bugs; correctness unchanged (`failed=0` both, idempotent warm rerun). See `KNOWN_MISSING.md`'s round-38 entry. |
| robertdebock.vault (+ robertdebock.hashicorp) | ✅ Clean (round 35, 0.9.382; same round as kubernetes above, same host pair). 1 more real bug: the `ansible_apparmor` fact was entirely unimplemented, so the role's own `when: ansible_apparmor.status == "enabled"` guard on its `aa-enforce` hardening task always evaluated false regardless of the host's real AppArmor state - real Ansible ran the task (this host has AppArmor active), this engine always silently skipped it. Fixed to match real Ansible's own `ApparmorFactCollector` exactly. Vault service verified `active` on both engines, idempotent (the role's own `aa-enforce` task is hardcoded `changed_when: true`, so `changed=1` every single rerun on both engines identically is by design, not a bug). See `KNOWN_MISSING.md`'s `0.9.382` entry. |

| robertdebock.dnsmasq (+ robertdebock.bootstrap) | ⚠️ Engine clean (round 49, no version bump) but `dnsmasq` itself can't start on this cloud image regardless of engine - `systemd-resolved`'s own stub listener already owns `127.0.0.53:53` on stock Ubuntu 22.04, and `dnsmasq --test` confirms the rendered config is syntactically valid on its own (pure port conflict, not a config bug). Everything up to that point matched task-for-task between engines. |
| robertdebock.squid (+ robertdebock.bootstrap) | ✅ Clean (round 48, 0.9.389). 1 real bug found and fixed: the role's own `assert.yml` has `squid_cache_dir.split(" ")[0] in [...]` (a Python-style `.split(...)` method call followed by indexing, in a bare `assert: that:` condition) - `ConditionalEvaluator`'s expression-routing guard didn't recognize a method call anywhere except a leading `(`, so it fell through to a naive dotted-path splitter that couldn't resolve it at all, always failing regardless of the real value. Widened the guard to any `(` in the expression. Live-reverified: `ok=21 changed=5 failed=0` matching real Ansible exactly, byte-identical `squid.conf`, `squid -k parse` valid, service `active`, fully idempotent warm rerun on both. See `KNOWN_MISSING.md`'s round-48 entry. |

Real bug found and fixed along the way: `robertdebock.java`'s own `assert.yml` uses `java_version \| int is number`/`in [...]` - a filter-chain type test - which `ConditionalEvaluator` treated as a literal (never-matching) variable name, always failing regardless of the real value. Generic fix (any `X \| filter is <type>` pattern was broken). See `KNOWN_MISSING.md`'s round-45 entry. |
| robertdebock.mongodb (+ robertdebock.bootstrap) | ⚠️ Engine clean (round 44, no version bump) but not runnable on this cloud CPU regardless of engine. A host/CPU-capability limitation, not an engine issue. |
| robertdebock.postgres (+ robertdebock.bootstrap) | ✅ Clean (round 43, 0.9.387). 4 real bugs found and fixed in sequence: `postgresql_db`/`postgresql_user`/`mysql_db`/`mysql_user` never recognized real Ansible's deprecated `name:` aliases (`db:`/`user:` respectively - the role's own tasks use exactly these); `postgresql_connection.cr` always forced a TCP connection to "localhost" instead of defaulting to a Unix socket the way real libpq does (this host's `pg_hba.conf` gates TCP behind `ident`, sockets behind `peer` - real Ansible connected fine, this plugin didn't); `login_host:`/`login_port:`/`login_user:`/`login_unix_socket:` never recognized their own deprecated aliases (`host:`/`port:`/`login:`/`unix_socket:` - the role sets a non-default `port: 6543` via the alias); and `postgresql_user`'s role creation used `CREATE ROLE` (implies `NOLOGIN`) instead of real Ansible's own `CREATE USER` (implies `LOGIN`), leaving created users unable to log in at all. Live-reverified: `\du` output identical on both engines, `rolcanlogin` confirmed `t` via direct SQL. See `KNOWN_MISSING.md`'s round-43 entry. |
| robertdebock.consul (+ robertdebock.bootstrap) | ⚠️ Engine clean (round 42, no version bump) but not fully runnable on this cloud image regardless of engine. Task-for-task identical on both engines throughout (`ok=28 changed=1 failed=0 skipped=5` both, byte-identical rendered `consul.hcl`). `consul` itself fails to start on BOTH engines identically - Ubuntu 22.04's apt-repo `consul` package is v1.8.7 (old), and the role's own rendered config uses a `license_path` key and `ui_config` block format that version doesn't understand at all (`invalid config key` from `consul agent`'s own parser, confirmed independent of Ansible) - a role/package-version mismatch, not a krikri-playbook issue. See `KNOWN_MISSING.md`'s round-42 entry for two other false leads (license-gated start task, a systemd start-limit race) ruled out along the way. |
| robertdebock.haproxy (+ robertdebock.bootstrap) | ✅ Clean (round 41, 0.9.386). 1 real bug found and fixed - in the vendored `weirdbricks/crinja` fork itself (tag `crystal-play-0.9.8`), not krikri-playbook proper: the role's own `haproxy.cfg.j2` template's `server.address | default(hostvars[server.name][...])` idiom crashed the WHOLE template render (not just that one lookup) whenever the `default()` fallback branch's own `hostvars[...]` chain hit a non-inventory host name, even though the fallback was never actually needed (`server.address` was already defined). Fixed to match real Ansible's own `Marker` class semantics (chained access on undefined self-propagates instead of raising; only an actually-*used* undefined value fails). Live-reverified: `haproxy.cfg` byte-identical, `haproxy -c` valid, service `active` on both engines, fully idempotent rerun. `community.general.seport`/`ansible.posix.seboolean` remain unimplemented (pre-existing scope gap, functionally identical skip on both engines on non-SELinux Ubuntu, just missing krikri-playbook's own `skipping:` banner). See `KNOWN_MISSING.md`'s round-41 entry. Round 134 (no version bump): re-ran cold+warm on a fresh pair, still byte-identical `haproxy.cfg`, valid, active, fully idempotent, no regression. |
| robertdebock.redis (+ robertdebock.bootstrap) | ✅ Clean (round 40, 0.9.385; first new role since round 35's vault/kubernetes). 1 real bug found and fixed on a fresh `G3.2GB` Atlantic.net pair: `mode: "{{ redis_mode }}"` (a templated string "640", no leading zero) got parsed as DECIMAL 640 by `template.cr`'s (and `copy.cr`'s/`ini_file.cr`'s) shared `starts_with?("0") ? octal : decimal` mode logic, producing octal `1200` (`--w------T`) instead of `0640` - `redis-server` couldn't even read its own config and crash-looped, while real `ansible-playbook` (which always parses an all-digit mode string as octal) came up clean. Fixed to match `file.cr`'s already-correct parsing. Live-reverified: `redis.conf` byte-identical content+mode on both engines, `redis-server` `active`/`PONG` on both, fully idempotent rerun (`ok=12 changed=0 failed=0 skipped=2` both). See `KNOWN_MISSING.md`'s round-40 entry. |
| devsec.hardening.nginx_hardening | ✅ Clean (round 39, no version bump - zero krikri-playbook bugs found). Fresh `G3.2GB` Atlantic.net pair, `geerlingguy.nginx` + `devsec.hardening.nginx_hardening`. Task-for-task match (cosmetic-only banner/`Gathering Facts` count diff, the usual artifact). Rerun showed `changed=9` (not idempotent) identically on **both** engines - traced to the hardening role's own `lineinfile`-style `nginx.conf` edits perpetually drifting against `geerlingguy.nginx`'s templated copy of the same file (a role-interaction quirk between two independently-authored roles, reproduced on real `ansible-playbook` too, not an engine bug), plus the two always-firing reload/restart handlers that follow. `nginx` `active` on both, config `nginx -t` valid on both (no vhost defined in this test playbook, so no `listen` socket on either engine - identical, not a divergence). |

| dev-sec.os_hardening (standalone role, `dev-sec/ansible-os-hardening`) | ✅ Clean as of 0.9.339 - see `git log`/prior round detail for that verification. Not the same code as the collection-form entry below; not re-tested since. |
| devsec.hardening.os_hardening (collection form, `dev-sec/ansible-collection-hardening`) | ✅ Re-verified clean again (0.9.635, Ubuntu 22.04, fresh pair + swapped-host control) as the workload for perf item 3 (batched groups over the daemon): identical recap cold (`ok=102 changed=36 failed=0 skipped=50`) and warm (`ok=95 changed=0 failed=0 skipped=52`) on both engines, warm 2.57x faster. Earlier: ✅ Re-verified clean (0.9.633, Ubuntu 22.04, fresh `G3.2GB` pair) as the workload for perf items 0-1. One real bug found and fixed in that pass: Jinja2's `in` TEST spelling (`item is not in os_always_ignore_users`, `user_accounts.yml`) was mis-split by the `not in` OPERATOR handler into an undefined `item is` operand, failing every looped item; verified against real ansible-core 2.19.4 before fixing. Post-fix live: `ok=95 changed=0 failed=0 skipped=52`, rc=0, idempotent. Earlier status: ✅ Fixed (0.9.487-0.9.488). Heaviest, most templating-dense role tested to date (100+ tasks). Two real bugs, found in sequence chasing a real-role performance benchmark: (1) a bare (non-`{{ }}`) `when: not lookup('varnames', ...)` condition never actually invoked the `lookup()` call at all, always evaluating the condition text as an undefined variable name - left `auditd_package` (and every other OS-family variable this role loads via a dynamic `set_fact:` loop) undefined, failing the role's very first real task; (2) task-level `vars:` were rendered unconditionally, before `when:` was even checked, so a `vars:` expression that legitimately raises (`| first` on a genuinely empty sequence, even with `| default(None)` right after it) crashed a task even when `when:` would have skipped it before real Ansible's own lazy per-key Jinja templating ever touched it - the role's mount-hardening task hits this for every disabled-by-default mount entry (6 of 9). Fixed with a narrow per-key rescue in `render_task_vars` (leaves the one failing key absent/undefined rather than crashing the whole task) rather than the broader lazy-Undefined redesign a first attempt at the filter layer needed and was reverted for (see `git log` `0.9.487`/`0.9.488`). Live-reverified on a real 2-host Atlantic.net `G3.2GB` pair (USEAST1, `--release`): `ok=102 changed=36 failed=0 skipped=50` cold (63.3s) vs real Python `ansible-playbook`'s `ok=101 changed=36 failed=0 skipped=50` (210.3s) - `ok` off-by-one is the already-documented `Gathering Facts` counting cosmetic; `changed=0` idempotent warm rerun on crystal (33.8s vs Python's 180.1s), real state verified on both hosts (`auditd`/sysctl hardening/`/etc/passwd` permissions), not just the recap. One residual, unexplained-not-investigated discrepancy noted rather than chased: `auditd` came up `active` on the crystal-side host but `inactive` on the python-side host post-run - plausibly the real, well-known "auditd needs a reboot to fully take effect" Linux quirk hitting one host's timing rather than an engine difference, but not confirmed either way; flag for a future round if it recurs. |
| konstruktoid.hardening | ❌ Not testable — role-side UFW lockout, reproduced in round 24. The role's `tasks/ufw.yml` enables the `ufw` systemd service (with its default `deny incoming` policy) **before** the `community.general.ufw` allow-rules tasks run, and a subsequent `Configure conntrack sysctl` block in `tasks/ufw.yml` reloads `net.netfilter.*` sysctls that flush the controller's persistent SSH connection through conntrack. Symptom: all new TCP connections (22, 80, anything) time out while ICMP keeps responding — textbook dropped-new-conns-after-conntrack-flush. Reproduced on a fresh 2-node `G3.2GB` Atlantic.net pair running real `ansible-playbook` (ansible-core 2.19.4 + `konstruktoid.hardening` v4.6.0 + `community.general` 12.5.0 from the role's pinned `requirements.yml`); lockout happened at ~2:24 elapsed / ~1:38 of useful work (the `Block blacklisted kernel modules` task in `tasks/kernelmodules.yml` was the last thing seen in the log before the controller's SSH went silent). **Both engines hit this identically** — the UFW lockout is in the role itself, not in any engine. The role's GitHub README explicitly says "the code is *not* idempotent and no longer actively maintained" and points users at the new `konstruktoid/ansible-collection-hardening` collection form; if we ever re-visit, the collection form is the more useful target, but until then the standalone role is a known-unrunnable environment, not a krikri-playbook gap. See KNOWN_MISSING.md's round-24 entry. |

| devsec.hardening.mysql_hardening (collection) | ✅ Clean cold pass (round 24 role 2, 0.9.348). The modern collection form of the dev-sec hardening family (FQCN `devsec.hardening`, v10.6.0 - the standalone `konstruktoid.hardening` role redirects here; the standalone `os_hardening` does NOT redirect and is still maintained as a separate role at `dev-sec/ansible-os-hardening`). Cold on a fresh 2-node `G3.2GB` Atlantic.net Ubuntu 22.04 pair (ansible-core 2.19.4 vs krikri-playbook HEAD): crystal `ok=22 changed=3 failed=0 skipped=6` vs python baseline `ok=22 changed=8 failed=0 skipped=6` - the ok/skipped counts are identical; the changed count differs by 5 due to three deferred warm-idempotency bugs (file-plugin `chown`/`chmod` not honoring `follow: true` on symlinks, so `Protect my.cnf` lchown'd the symlink instead of chowning the target `/etc/mysql/mariadb.cnf` - leaving it at `root:root` instead of `root:mysql`; `mysql_user` reporting `changed: Updated user root` on every warm rerun when the password is already set; and the chmod side of the same symlink-following issue). Two real engine bugs fixed in this round: `role_loader.cr` `File.expand_path("~/.ansible/collections")` not expanding `~` (silently masking the default collections lookup for any CWD other than `$HOME` - same tilde-expansion bug class already fixed for `~/.my.cnf` in 0.9.346 and `BasePlugin#expand_tilde`), and `with_community.general.flattened` pushing the engine's "no value" sentinels (`"undefined"`, `""`, `"[]"`, `"{}"`) as one loop item (the role's "Ensure that there are no users without password" task tried `DROP USER undefined@%` on a fresh MariaDB). Also exercises the new `dict2items`/`items2dict` filters from 0.9.347 end-to-end via the role's own `mysql_hardening_options \| dict2items \| rejectattr('key', 'search', 'secure-auth') \| list \| items2dict` chain, working correctly in this live context. The three deferred warm-idempotency bugs (file-plugin `follow:` symlink handling, `mysql_user` `host_all:` idempotency) plus a `with_flattened` literal-source regression found while fixing them were addressed in `0.9.349`. Round 25 live-reverified all of it on a fresh `G3.2GB` pair plus found and fixed one more (`mysql_query` reporting `changed` on a 0-row DML statement) - warm rerun now `changed=0` on crystal, matching python's `changed=0` exactly (`0.9.350`, see `KNOWN_MISSING.md`). |

| *(not a Galaxy role - targeted feature verification)* `docker_container`/`firewalld` | ✅ Clean (round 34, 0.9.378; first round since round 33's nomad). Not a role round - a custom playbook targeted at live-verifying `0.9.375`-`0.9.377`'s new `firewalld: port_forward:` and `docker_container: ports:`/`healthcheck:`/comparison-default work end to end via the real playbook-execution engine, on request, after those features had only been verified via direct plugin-binary invocation. Fresh `G3.2GB` Atlantic.net pair, real Docker Engine 29.1.3 (notably different from the Podman used to build the features) + firewalld installed fresh on both. Found and fixed 3 real bugs only reachable against genuine Docker Engine: two more `docr` fork nullability crashes (`Image#graph_driver`, alongside `ContainerInspectResponse#graph_driver`/`Health#log` found in the same session - Docker Engine 29.1.3 omits/nulls fields older/other-daemon inspect responses always populated) and a real daemon-API-version-dependent `HostIp` difference (`""` under this plugin's own unversioned/latest-API client vs `"0.0.0.0"` under real Ansible's own older pinned-API client) that silently recreated any container with `ports:` set on every single rerun - confirmed by live-patching the installed `community.docker` collection in place to dump its own actual/expected comparison values. Final result identical on both engines: `ok=20 changed=5 failed=0`, every assertion (idempotency, drift-detection, `comparisons: ignore`, container health, firewalld `port_forward` add/idempotent/remove) passed. See `KNOWN_MISSING.md`'s `0.9.378` entry. |
| robertdebock.nomad (+ robertdebock.hashicorp) | ⚠️ Engine clean (round 33, 0.9.363-0.9.365; first new role since round 32's nextcloud) but the role/Nomad-v2.0.5 combo can't fully succeed on this cloud host regardless of engine. 3 real krikri-playbook bugs found and fixed: `conditional_evaluator.cr` had no fallback for ANY unimplemented Jinja2 `is [not] <test>` (divisibleby, even, odd, equalto, sameas, etc) - always evaluated false regardless of the real outcome (fixed with a generic Crinja-delegation fallback); `apt_repository:`'s post-add `apt-get update` failure was silently swallowed instead of failing the task, breaking the role's own `block:`/`rescue:` GPG-key recovery pattern (fixed to propagate the failure, then fixed AGAIN to roll back the just-written repo line on that failure, avoiding a second-order "conflicting Signed-By" apt error from the rescue's retry). Cold pass `ok=26 changed=7 failed=1 skipped=13 rescued=1` vs python `ok=24 changed=0 failed=1 skipped=13 rescued=1`, task-by-task identical (cosmetic-only diff). Both engines hit the SAME expected external failure at "List server members" - Nomad's HTTP API doesn't bind 127.0.0.1 once `bind_addr` is a specific host IP (needed to work around a stale `ansible_default_ipv4` fact on this Atlantic.net host, itself needed because Nomad v2.0.5 won't start at all with the role's own default `0.0.0.0` bind_addr) - a real role/Nomad-version/CLI-default incompatibility, not an engine issue. See `KNOWN_MISSING.md` 0.9.363-0.9.365 for full detail. |
| robertdebock.nextcloud | ✅ Clean (round 32, 0.9.360-0.9.362; first new role since round 31's ssh_hardening). 3 real bugs found and fixed on a fresh `G3.2GB` Atlantic.net pair: `command:`'s `chdir:` crashed an otherwise-successful task under `become_user:` (tried to restore the process's original cwd afterward, which is `/root` on a fresh SSH connection and inaccessible to an unprivileged become_user - fixed by removing the pointless restore); `unarchive:`'s `owner:`/`group:`/`mode:` only applied to the destination directory itself instead of recursively (broke the role's own downstream `occ` commands needing the whole extracted tree web-server-owned - fixed with `chown -R`/`chgrp -R`/`chmod -R`); `package:`'s virtual/Provides:-satisfied package names (`php-dom`/`php-posix`, resolved via Provides: to `php8.1-xml`/`php8.1-common`, not real dpkg entries) always reported `changed` forever on warm reruns - same bug class already fixed in `apt.cr` for the ruby round but never ported to this separate `package:` code path, now ported. Cold pass `ok=27 changed=11 failed=0 skipped=6` vs python `ok=26 changed=11 failed=0 skipped=7` (cosmetic-only diff). Warm rerun fully idempotent on both engines (`changed=0`). Nextcloud verified reachable and functional (`curl` 200, correct www-data ownership recursively). See `KNOWN_MISSING.md` 0.9.360-0.9.362. Round 134 (no version bump): re-ran cold+warm on a fresh pair, `curl` 200 and ownership byte-identical on both, fully idempotent, no regression (an initial apache2 start failure was port 80 already held by an earlier `haproxy` role test reusing the same host pair - self-inflicted, not an engine bug). |
| devsec.hardening.ssh_hardening | ✅ Clean (round 31, 0.9.359; first new role since round 30's prometheus). 1 real bug found and fixed on a fresh `G3.2GB` Atlantic.net pair: `regex_replace`'s `\1`/`\2` backreference handling was backwards - it rewrote Python-style backreferences to `$1`/`$2` on the mistaken assumption Crystal's `gsub(Regex, String)` used Ruby-style `$`-backreferences (it doesn't; Crystal already matches Python's `\1` syntax directly), so the "translated" replacement was emitted completely literally. Broke the role's own `ssh -V` output parsing, cascading into 3 undefined openssh-version-gated `set_fact:` variables and a downstream loop crash. Cold pass `ok=34 changed=5 failed=0 skipped=5` vs python `ok=41 changed=8 failed=0 skipped=4` (task-by-task diff cosmetic-only). Both hosts locked out of root SSH identically post-play (role's own `ssh_permit_root_login: "no"` default, expected since we ran as root - not an engine bug, see `KNOWN_MISSING.md` 0.9.359 for detail). |
| prometheus.prometheus.prometheus | ✅ Clean (round 30, 0.9.358; first new role since round 28's pushgateway follow-up). 2 real bugs found and fixed on a fresh `G3.2GB` Atlantic.net pair: `fileglob`/`realpath` Jinja filters entirely missing from both evaluators (broke the role's own alert-rules-file-copy loop), and a Crinja fork parser bug where multi-arg parenthesized `is name(a, b)` TEST calls never split their arguments into separate positional args - broke the role's own `is version('2.7.0', '>=')` flag-selection guard, causing real prometheus to crash-loop on the deprecated `--storage.tsdb.retention` flag. Fixed in the `weirdbricks/crinja` fork itself (tag `crystal-play-0.9.7`), not just krikri-playbook. Cold pass `ok=38 changed=10 failed=0 skipped=14` vs python `ok=42 changed=14 failed=0 skipped=14` (count diff from scp-pre-staged binaries during investigation, task-by-task diff cosmetic-only). Idempotent, service verified live (`systemctl is-active` + `curl :9090/-/healthy` 200). See `KNOWN_MISSING.md` 0.9.358. |
| prometheus.prometheus.pushgateway | ✅ Clean (round 28, 0.9.356; `notify:` handler gap closed in 0.9.357 and live-reverified in a follow-up round on a fresh host pair). Round 28 found and fixed a real gap in 0.9.353's own task-name-rendering fix: round 26's eager include_role task-name substitution permanently baked in "undefined" for a task name referencing a var not explicitly passed via `include_role: vars:` (`_common_service_name`, computed internally) - removed entirely, fully superseded by 0.9.353's lazy render. 0.9.357 then fixed the deferred `notify:`-substitution gap (`_common`'s generic cross-role handler notification never firing) plus two related bugs found in the same investigation. **Live-reverified**: cold pass now correctly fires the restart handler with real output; a deliberate config-only-change warm rerun (forcing `pushgateway_web_telemetry_path`) confirmed the exact scenario the bug broke - the direct "enabled on boot" task reports `ok` (not `changed`, matching python), and the handler alone fires and restarts the service, verified via `systemctl status` (fresh restart timestamp) and `curl` against the new config path. See `KNOWN_MISSING.md` 0.9.356/0.9.357. |
| prometheus.prometheus.blackbox_exporter | ✅ Clean (round 27, 0.9.352). 3 real gaps found and fixed on a fresh `G3.2GB` Atlantic.net pair: `package.cr`'s `name:` parsing had the same Python-repr-list JSON gap as `apt.cr` (independent copy, actually the code path this role hits since it uses `ansible.builtin.package:`), and `community.general.capabilities` had no plugin at all (new `capabilities.cr`, ported from the real Python module's `getcap`/`setcap` parsing). Cold pass `ok=31 changed=9 failed=0 skipped=5` vs python `ok=34 changed=12 failed=0 skipped=7` (count diff is the known fact-cache artifact, not a real divergence - every task's status matched 1:1). `failed=0` on both, service verified `active` and answering `curl :9115`. Same cosmetic role-vars-task-name gap from round 26 observed again, not fixed. See `KNOWN_MISSING.md` 0.9.352. |
| prometheus.prometheus.alertmanager | ✅ Clean (round 26, 0.9.351). 7 real engine bugs found and fixed on a fresh `G3.2GB` Atlantic.net pair: dict-literal `== {}` comparison always false, `include_role: vars:` not re-rendering task names (also caused a real functional skip, not just cosmetic), `check_mode:`/`diff:` templated-value parse crash that silently dropped a whole task file (most severe - lost the task that writes alertmanager's own config), missing `version_compare`/`any`/`all`/`eq`-`lt`-`le`-`gt`-`ge`/`quote` Jinja tests and filters. Cold pass `ok=32 changed=8 failed=0 skipped=19` vs python `ok=35 changed=13 failed=0 skipped=20` (count diffs are a known fact-cache artifact plus a noted, non-blocking `copy:` re-upload inefficiency, not correctness issues) - `failed=0` on both, every expected task succeeded. One cosmetic-only gap left open (role vars/main.yml-sourced task names stay unrendered in banners). The role's own rendered systemd unit crash-loops real alertmanager 0.33.1 itself (`-cluster.listen-address=` empty-value CLI parsing bug) - reproduced independent of Ansible entirely, not an engine issue. See `KNOWN_MISSING.md` 0.9.351. Round 134 (`0.9.429`-`0.9.431`): 2 more real bugs found and fixed. `plugins/facts.cr` never set the flat `ansible_facts['python_version']` fact at all (missing since round 133's rewrite, even though it legitimately co-exists with the nested `ansible_python` dict in real Ansible) - the role's own `_common_dependencies` var does `ansible_facts['python_version'] is version('3', '<')` to pick `python-apt` vs `python3-apt`, and with the fact undefined the version test silently evaluated true, crashing the first real task by trying to install the nonexistent `python-apt` package. Also, `task_executor/executor.cr`'s single-host `run_task_list` printed a spurious empty `TASK [...]` banner for a named `block:` nested inside another `block:` (the role's own `_common`'s "Download binary {{ }}"/"Verify checksum of {{ }}" blocks) - blocks are transparent in real Ansible, only their members get banners; the multi-host path already had this guard, the single-host path didn't. Also fixed (same round) `unarchive.cr`'s `tar_changed?` unconditionally treating `tar --compare`'s Mode/Uid/Gid-differs lines as real changes even when the task's own `mode:`/`owner:`/`group:` explicitly overrides them - the role's own `mode: 0755` unarchive task was permanently non-idempotent (`changed: true` every rerun) because the archive's embedded member mode (0644) always differed from the requested 0755; fixed to match real Ansible's own `TgzArchive#is_unarchived` gating exactly. Live-reverified after each fix: `alertmanager` active, `curl` 200, recap `ok=34 changed=13 skipped=19` cold matching real Ansible's `changed=13`/`skipped=19` exactly, and fully idempotent warm rerun (`changed=0`) on both engines. |

| robertdebock.gnome | ✅ Clean (round 143, after fixing 2 real bugs in `package.cr`'s dnf backend: a single `@Group Name` (dnf comps-group syntax, e.g. Rocky 9.6's own `@Server with GUI` default) was silently mangled into 3 bogus space-split tokens, and even after that fix the group's own idempotency check used `rpm -q` which has no concept of a dnf group at all, always reporting `changed: true` forever. Fixed via a `single_name` tracking flag through the dnf/apt handlers plus a new `dnf group list installed`-based `dnf_group_installed?` check. | buluma.influxdb2 | ✅ Fixed (round 155, 0.9.505). First real-host test of InfluxDB 2.x install/setup, Rocky 9.6. One real bug found and fixed: `command.cr`'s `parse_command` treated every unquoted `\` uniformly as "escape the next character" (shlex semantics), but the role's own `command: influx ping \ --host "{{ influxdb_host }}"` uses a documented, intentional Ansible authoring convention - a bare `\` token (whitespace on both sides) as a *line-continuation marker* for writing a long `command:` as if it were multiple lines. Real Ansible's task-arg parser (`ansible.parsing.splitter.split_args`, run before Jinja templating) drops a standalone `\` token entirely and rejoins the remaining words with single spaces (`influx ping --host <url>`); crystal's old uniform escape rule instead absorbed the escaped space into the current token, producing a malformed `" --host"` argv element (stray leading space) that real `influx`'s cobra-based CLI parser rejected outright as an unknown subcommand - while real ansible-playbook succeeded. Verified directly against `ansible.parsing.splitter.split_args`/`join_args` and a real `influx` binary (both confirm the exact mechanism) before fixing. Live-reverified on a fresh pair: byte-identical `ok=14 changed=11 failed=0 skipped=7` cold and `ok=13 changed=0 failed=0 skipped=7` warm on both engines. Crystal warm 12.3× faster (3.56s vs 43.78s). |

| buluma.netdata | ✅ Re-fixed (round 64002, 0.9.816). Found via the 200-role regression round: `changed_when:`/`failed_when:` couldn't see the task's own `stdout_lines`/`stderr_lines` (register_result's augmentation only reached LATER tasks, not the task's own eval_context) - the role's own "install | Use netdata dependencies installation." task's `changed_when: ... not in netdata_requirements_install.stderr_lines` raised "object of type 'dict' has no attribute 'stderr_lines'". Fixed by sharing the augmentation logic between both call sites. Confirm round (0.9.817): the fixed task itself now succeeds identically to real Ansible (`changed:`, no failure) on both engines; the role's LATER, unrelated `netdata-installer.sh` exits 1 on this specific run (a real build/resource issue on this ~24-minute-build role - real ansible-playbook's own cold run didn't even finish within the confirm harness's 900s timeout, so there's no valid comparison at that later step). Earlier: ✅ Fixed (round 156, 0.9.506). One real bug found and fixed: `git.cr`'s `clone` shallow-cloned only the default branch's tip (`git clone --depth N <repo>`) and then tried `git checkout <version>` against that limited history - the role's own `depth: 1, version: v1.44.0` task failed with "pathspec 'v1.44.0' did not match any file(s) known to git" (the tag was never fetched at all), while real ansible-playbook succeeded in ~24 minutes (its own git module fetches a targeted refspec for the requested ref at the given depth instead of just shallow-cloning the default branch - confirmed directly against `ansible-core`'s own `modules/git.py` `fetch()` source). Fixed by having `git clone` itself take `--branch <version>` for the depth+non-HEAD case (falling back to a full clone+checkout when `version:` is a commit sha, not a branch/tag). Given the full role's real cold run takes ~24 minutes even on real Ansible (a full netdata source build), the fix was live-reverified with an isolated single-task repro (the exact failing `git:` call, same repo/tag/depth) rather than the full role: byte-identical `rc=0` cold AND warm on both engines. |

| buluma.ssh_keys | ✅ Fixed (round 156, 0.9.506). One real bug found and fixed: no String method-call handling existed for Python's `.lstrip()`/`.rstrip()`/`.strip()` at all - the role's own `src: "{{ ssh_keys_known_hosts_path.lstrip('/') }}.j2"` collapsed the whole `{{ }}` to the literal text "undefined", so `template:`'s `src:` became the nonexistent path "undefined.j2" ("Template file not found on controller"). Added with correct Python char-SET semantics (`"xxyx".lstrip("xy")` strips every leading x/y, not just a literal "xy" prefix - verified against real Python). Live-reverified on a fresh pair: byte-identical `ok=3 changed=1 failed=0 skipped=12` cold and `ok=3 changed=0 failed=0 skipped=12` warm on both engines. Crystal warm 7.2× faster (1.28s vs 9.22s). |
| buluma.checkmk_agent | ✅ Regression root-caused and fixed (round 65125/65304 found it, live-reverified on a fresh Kata VM, 0.9.831): a `delegate_to: localhost, failed_when: false` task inheriting `become: true` needed a sudo password on the controller it didn't have - real Ansible aborts the whole play as `fatal:` unconditionally (a become/connection failure has no module result for `failed_when:` to reinterpret), but this engine let `failed_when: false` suppress it and continued. Fixed by tagging every "no real module result" `PluginResult` and having `failed_when:`/`changed_when:` skip entirely when it's set. Regression spec added (`spec/integration/connection_failure_unignorable_by_failed_when_spec.cr`). Live-reverified: `ok=14 changed=5 failed=1`, matching real Ansible's `ok=14 changed=6 failed=1` baseline. Earlier: ⚠️ Regression, inverted, not yet root-caused (round 65125/65304, reproduced identically twice): krikri succeeded (`ok=17 failed=0`) where real Ansible failed (`ok=14 failed=1`). Earlier still: ✅ Fixed (round 156, 0.9.506). One real bug found and fixed: `gather_network_facts` populated `ansible_facts['default_ipv4']` with `address`/`interface` but never `gateway` at all - the role's own `when: ansible_facts['default_ipv4'].gateway is defined` was always false (the key genuinely didn't exist), silently skipping a debug task real Ansible ran. Fixed by also parsing the gateway IP (the `via` field) out of `ip -4 route get 1`'s output. Both engines still hit the same later, unrelated `failed=1` (an RPM package install issue independent of this fix) - `ok=11 changed=4 failed=1 skipped=3` (py) / `ok=10 changed=4 failed=1 skipped=4` (cr, before the fix - the recap gap is exactly this one task) cold, `ok=11/ok=10 changed=0 failed=1` warm. Crystal warm 3.8× faster (5.19s vs 19.47s). |

| buluma.mediawiki | ✅ Fixed (round 156, 0.9.506). Depends on `buluma.httpd` (auto-installed). Two real bugs found and fixed: (1) same missing-`gateway`-fact class as `buluma.checkmk_agent` above, harmless here (no `when:` depends on it); (2) `package.cr`'s `handle_dnf` (and independently, `dnf.cr`'s and `yum.cr`'s own `package_installed?`) checked installed status with a plain `rpm -q <name>` / `dnf list installed <name>` - the role's own `package: name: [php-intl, php-json, php-mbstring, php-mysqlnd, php-xml]` includes `php-json`, which on RHEL 9's PHP 8.0 packaging is pure `Provides:` from `php-common` (JSON is bundled into PHP core) with no `php-json` RPM of its own - neither check ever found it "installed", so every rerun re-"installed" it and reported `changed: true` forever, never converging (verified live in a Rocky 9 container: `rpm -q php-json` and `dnf list installed php-json` both report nothing, `rpm -q --whatprovides php-json` correctly resolves to `php-common`). Fixed by switching all three checks to `rpm -q --whatprovides`, matching how real Ansible's python-dnf-backed resolution treats virtual packages. Both engines still hit the same later, unrelated `failed=1` (the role's own `unzip` binary missing) - `ok=22 changed=5 failed=1 skipped=25` cold, `ok=20 changed=0 failed=1 skipped=25` (py) / `ok=20 changed=1 failed=1 skipped=23` (cr, before the fix - warm `changed=1` was exactly this bug; `skipped` gap is the pre-existing documented cosmetic parse-time-drop class, `community.general.seport`/`apache2_module` unimplemented) warm. Crystal warm 4.7× faster (5.98s vs 27.99s). Two real bugs found and fixed: (1) `package.cr`'s `handle_dnf` (and `dnf.cr`'s/`yum.cr`'s own `parse_package_names`) only ever read the `name:` param, never the documented `pkg:` alias (`aliases: [pkg]` in real Ansible's own dnf/yum module argument_spec) - the role's own `package: {pkg: "{{ item }}", state: present}` failed outright with "Missing required parameter: name" for every package in the loop; (2) a genuine Crinja lexer bug: the role's own `etc_named.conf.j2` has a U+00A0 NO-BREAK SPACE (a common copy/paste artifact) right after `{{` in `dnssec-validation {{ bind_dnssec_validation }};` - real ansible-playbook (Python's Unicode-mode `re`) renders it fine, but Crinja's lexer checked a fixed ASCII-only whitespace set, corrupting the expression parse ("Not implemented expression value"). Fixed upstream in the krikri-playbook fork of the crinja shard (`weirdbricks/crinja`, tag bumped `crystal-play-0.9.13` → `crystal-play-0.9.14`) by switching to Crystal's own Unicode-aware `Char#whitespace?`. Live-reverified on a fresh pair: byte-identical `rc=0` cold and warm on both engines. Crystal warm 8.2× faster (5.27s vs 43.14s). |

| buluma.victoriametrics | ✅ Fixed (round 157, 0.9.507). One real bug found and fixed: `ExpressionEvaluator#fetch_url_lines` (backing `lookup('url', ...)`) silently returned `"undefined"` on any non-2xx HTTP response instead of failing - the role's own `set_fact: _checksums: "{{ lookup('url', ...) }}"` hit a real 404 (a stale `victoriametrics_version` default whose GitHub release checksums file has since been removed - broken-upstream, not this engine's doing), and real ansible-playbook fails that task right there ("The lookup plugin 'url' failed: Received HTTP error ... 404"). Silently degrading let crystal's execution continue into a `with_items:` loop over a single bogus `"undefined"` item, producing a real `ok=`/`skipped=` recap divergence even though both engines ultimately failed the role overall. Fixed by raising on a non-2xx response, matching real Ansible; that surfaced a SECOND, more serious bug the fix itself exposed - nothing in the task executor's call chain caught the raised exception, so it crashed the ENTIRE process with an unhandled-exception stack trace instead of failing just the one task. Fixed by wrapping task-argument substitution in `execute_task_once` with a rescue that converts any such exception into a normal failed-task result (exit code 2, clean recap), matching real Ansible's own "fail this task, keep clean process semantics" behavior. Live-reverified on a fresh pair (twice - the crash fix needed its own extra round): byte-identical `ok=6 changed=3 failed=1 skipped=4` cold and `ok=6 changed=0 failed=1 skipped=4` warm on both engines, no crash. Crystal warm 8.0× faster (2.54s vs 20.31s). |

One real bug found and fixed: `playbook_parser.cr`'s `extract_command_special_params` (parsing a bare-string `command:`/`shell:` task for trailing `creates=`/`removes=`/`chdir=`/`executable=` params) unconditionally tokenized the WHOLE raw string and rejoined the surviving tokens with `.join(" ")` - even when there were no trailing special params to strip at all, this collapsed every real newline in a multi-statement `shell:` string into a single space. The role's own `shell: "set -euo pipefail\n{{ cfssl_bin_directory }}/cfssl gencert -initca ca-consul-csr.json | {{ cfssl_bin_directory }}/cfssljson -bare ca-consul\n"` (a common readability idiom) became one line, `set -euo pipefail <path>/cfssl gencert ... | ...cfssljson ...` - `set` just assigns its trailing words as positional parameters and does NOT execute them, so the actual `cfssl gencert | cfssljson` pipeline never ran; `cfssljson` then read empty stdin and failed with "unexpected end of JSON input" - while real ansible-playbook (which never rejoins/re-tokenizes the command string this way) ran it correctly. Fixed by tracking each token's end offset during tokenization and slicing the ORIGINAL string up to the first stripped special param instead of reassembling from tokens, preserving all original whitespace/newlines; the existing trailing-special-param-stripping behavior (verified against `iptables -F creates=/etc/firewall.bash`-style tasks) is unchanged. Live-reverified on a fresh pair: byte-identical `rc=0` cold and warm on both engines. Crystal warm 5.0× faster (8.22s vs 40.70s). |

Real ansible's own cold run took over 8.5 minutes (a genuinely slow role, not a benchmark artifact). Crystal warm 23.0× faster (12.73s vs 292.62s) - the largest speedup of the round. |

| linux-system-roles.timesync | ✅ Regression mostly fixed (round 65208/65402 found it, live-reverified on a fresh Kata VM, 0.9.829): same root causes as linux-system-roles.logging's own regression (see `KNOWN_MISSING.md`'s 0.9.829 correction) - `sr_fingerprint` now runs correctly. Fixed by making `resolve_role_dir`'s result always absolute (`File.expand_path`) at the one place `task.role_path` gets set, fixing every downstream consumer (role_files_dir/role_templates_dir/role_vars_dir derive from the same value) at once. Live-reverified on a fresh pair: byte-identical `rc=0` cold and warm on both engines. Crystal warm 7.8× faster (8.28s vs 64.82s). |

Fixed by adding the same exit-code check. No spec (needs a real broken-apt host); verified live pre/post-fix on the same host. Earlier still: ✅ Fixed (round 158, 0.9.509). Two real bugs found and fixed: (1) the SAME `role_path`-absolutization bug as the two roles above (`lookup('first_found', ...)` at "Gather variables for each operating system"); (2) `debug.cr`'s own verbosity: gate already correctly detected a run below the required verbosity and returned `skipped: true` in its `PluginResult` - but `PluginResult` has no real `skipped` FIELD, so that `skipped: true` kwarg landed in its generic `@extra` bag and got serialized as an ordinary JSON key nothing downstream ever checked; since changed/failed both stayed false it fell into the normal "ok" display/stats path, printing "ok: [host]" followed by the plugin's own literal msg text ("skipped") as if it were real debug output, and counting it under `ok=` instead of `skipped=` (the role's own 4 `debug: verbosity: 2` "Debug role vars" tasks). Fixed by having `finish_single_task` check for a plugin-reported `skipped` result and route it through the same `skipping:`/`skipped=` path as a `when:`-false skip. Both engines still hit the same later, unrelated `failed=1` ("Ensuring keepalived is enabled and started" - a real systemd timeout starting the service on a bare fresh host, external/environmental, not an engine bug). Live-reverified on a fresh pair: byte-identical `ok=11 changed=4 failed=1 skipped=22` cold AND warm on both engines. Crystal warm 1.35× faster (93.73s vs 126.93s - the smallest speedup of the round, since most of the wall-clock on both engines is the real systemd start timeout, not task dispatch). |

Fixed by trying a plain `rpm -q <name>` FIRST in all three plugins (correctly matches bare names AND NEVRA specifiers), falling back to `--whatprovides` only when that fails (still catches round 156's virtual-package case, e.g. `php-json`). | linux-system-roles.storage | ⚠️ Regression partially fixed (round 65210/65403 found it, live-reverified on a fresh Kata VM, 0.9.829): same three arbitrary-Python-module bugs as linux-system-roles.logging's regression (see `KNOWN_MISSING.md`'s 0.9.829 correction) all fixed - `sr_fingerprint` and the package-install step both now succeed, well past the original `ok=13 failed=1` stopping point. Still ultimately fails: the role's own `blivet:` module imports a custom `ansible.module_utils.storage_lsr` package this engine doesn't bundle (`ModuleNotFoundError`) - a new, separate, larger scope gap (arbitrary-module-utils bundling), documented under "Deliberate limits" in `KNOWN_MISSING.md`, not fixed here. Earlier: ⚠️ Regression, not yet root-caused (round 65210/65403, reproduced identically twice): krikri stopped much earlier than real Ansible (`ok=13 failed=1` vs real Ansible's `ok=21 failed=0`) - previously reached a clean `ok=18 changed=3 failed=0`. Earlier still: ✅ Fixed (round 159, 0.9.511). Official RHEL system role. One real bug found and fixed: `include_vars:` with a real `loop:` (as opposed to `with_first_found:`, the only loop form it ever supported) was dispatched to its own dedicated executor method BEFORE the generic task-loop machinery ever ran, so the whole task executed exactly once with `item` never bound at all - the role's own "Set platform/version specific variables" (`include_vars: "{{ role_path }}/vars/{{ item }}", loop: ["{{ os_family }}.yml", "{{ distribution }}.yml", "{{ distribution }}_{{ major_version }}.yml", "{{ distribution }}_{{ version }}.yml"], when: __vars_file is file`) never loaded `vars/Rocky_9.yml`, leaving `blivet_package_list` undefined - "Make sure blivet is available" then failed outright trying to install the package literally named "undefined". Fixed by giving `execute_include_vars` its own per-item loop (binding `item`, re-rendering the task's `vars:` against each binding the same way `execute_looped_task` already does, evaluating `when:` per item, and aggregating ok/skipped/failed once for the whole task like every other loop). Live-reverified on a single diagnostic host: `Rocky_9.yml` now loads correctly (skipping `RedHat.yml`/`Rocky.yml`/`Rocky_9.6.yml`, matching the file's actual name), and the role proceeds to a clean `ok=18 changed=3 failed=0` (previously `rc=2` on every run, cold and warm). |
| linux-system-roles.logging | ✅ Regression root-caused and fixed (round 65131/65307 found it, live-reverified on a fresh Kata VM, 0.9.829 - SUPERSEDES this row's earlier "not a regression, distro-mismatch" note, which was itself wrong): the `rc=4 ok=28 skipped=53` vs real Ansible's `rc=0 ok=30 skipped=51` gap was the role's own "Record role success fingerprint" task, a genuinely self-contained new-style Python module (`sr_fingerprint`) in the role's own `library/` dir that SHOULD have run via the 0.9.819 arbitrary-Python-module feature but didn't, due to three independent, compounding bugs in that feature (role-root resolution used `role_files_dir`, only set when a role ships `files/`; new-style module args were passed via a nonexistent `ANSIBLE_MODULE_ARGS` env var real ansible-core 2.19 doesn't read; task-batching had no exclusion for an unavailable-module task) - see `KNOWN_MISSING.md`'s 0.9.829 correction narrative for the full story. All three fixed; live-reverified byte-identical `ok=30 changed=2 skipped=51 failed=0`. Earlier: ✅ Fixed (round 159, 0.9.512). Official RHEL system role. One real bug found and fixed: `render_include_role_vars` (the `include_role: vars:` renderer) evaluated each `vars:` entry one at a time straight against the ORIGINAL vars_context, with no visibility into its OWN sibling entries from the same `vars:` block - a Hash has no meaningful evaluation order to Jinja, but this gave first-declared-first-rendered semantics. The role's own `include_role: name: "{{ role_path }}/roles/rsyslog", vars: {rsyslog_custom_config_files: "{{ __custom_config_files + logging_custom_config_files }}", __custom_config_files: "{{ logging_outputs | d([]) | ... | flatten | list }}"}` declares `rsyslog_custom_config_files` BEFORE the `__custom_config_files` it depends on - `__custom_config_files` rendered as undefined, and the `+` concatenation silently mis-rendered to the STRING `"[]"` instead of a real empty array; piped through `| flatten` downstream (`loop: '{{ rsyslog_custom_config_files | flatten }}'`), that 2-character string was iterated CHARACTER BY CHARACTER, producing two bogus loop items `"["` and `"]"`, each fed to `copy: src: "{{ item }}"` as a nonexistent file path ("Source file not found: ["). Fixed by seeding a scratch context with every sibling's raw (still-unrendered) value BEFORE rendering any of them, mirroring how a regular task's own `vars:` already resolves cross-references via `build_vars_context` + `render_task_vars`. Live-reverified on a single diagnostic host: the bogus loop items are gone and the role reaches a clean `ok=30 changed=0 failed=0` (previously `rc=2`, `failed=1` on the `[`/`]` items, on every run cold and warm). |

| ahuffman.resolv | ✅ Fixed (round 159, crinja `crystal-play-0.9.15` + krikri-playbook 0.9.510). One real bug found and fixed, in the vendored Crinja fork: iterating a `{% for %}` loop over an undefined variable used `StrictUndefined` to decide whether to raise `UndefinedError`, silently iterating a plain (non-strict) `Undefined` as an empty sequence instead - real Ansible's own Jinja2 environment raises for EITHER, with no lenient case for iteration specifically (verified directly: real ansible-playbook fails the whole role with `'resolv_nameservers' is undefined`). The role's own `resolv.conf.j2` guards `resolv_search`/`resolv_domain`/`resolv_sortlist`/`resolv_options` with `is defined` before use in the same template, but not `resolv_nameservers` - krikri-playbook previously rendered the file with that section silently empty and reported success (`rc=0`) where real Ansible correctly fails (`rc=2`). Fixed both `Crinja::Value#raw_each` overloads to raise for `Undefined` the same way they already did for `StrictUndefined`; updated 4 of Crinja's own for-tag/value specs (ported from vanilla pallets/jinja, which does NOT raise here - Ansible's environment is stricter than vanilla Jinja2 for this one operation) to match the verified-correct Ansible behavior instead. Live-reverified with a local repro matching the role's exact template/failure text; the krikri-playbook side of the original round-159 divergence (`rc=0` where it should have been `rc=2`) is resolved. |
| riemers.gitlab-runner | ✅ Re-fixed (round 64004, 0.9.817). Found via the 200-role regression round: `loop_control.index_var` never reached an included file's own tasks - `run_include_tasks_once` propagated the loop item and `loop_var` into each included task's own vars, but never `index_var`. The role's own "Write config section for each runner" (`loop_control: {loop_var: runner_config, index_var: runner_config_index}`, `include_tasks: config-runner.yml`) rendered its OWN name/vars: fine (masking the gap), but `config-runner.yml`'s first task's `prefix: gitlab-runner.{{ runner_config_index }}.` failed with "'runner_config_index' is undefined". Fixed by also copying index_var's bound value into the included task's vars. Confirm round (0.9.817): byte-identical `ok=40 changed=8 failed=0 skipped=19` cold, `ok=41 changed=0` warm, both engines. Earlier: ✅ Fixed (round 159, 0.9.513). One real bug found and fixed: `lineinfile:`'s `backrefs: true` mode used `String#gsub(Regex, String)` to substitute the matched line, which only replaces the SPAN the regexp actually matched and leaves whatever wasn't matched appended verbatim - but real Ansible's own backrefs semantics treat `line:` as a template for the ENTIRE line (Python's `match.expand(line)`, replacing the whole line, not just the matched substring). The role's own "Set concurrent option" (`regexp: ^(\s*)concurrent =`, `line: \1concurrent = {{ gitlab_runner_concurrent }}`, `backrefs: true` against an existing `concurrent = 1`) only matches the `concurrent =` prefix - gsub replaced that prefix and left the un-matched ` 1` tail in place, corrupting `config.toml` to `concurrent = 5 1` (invalid TOML), which then made gitlab-runner itself fail to parse its own config on every subsequent run (`FATAL: decoding configuration file: toml: ... but got '1' instead`) - a warm-only divergence, since the cold run's own first write already produced the corrupt file on both the cold AND warm passes but only the CLI-parse failure surfaced as a task failure once something tried to read it back. Fixed by expanding `line:`'s own backreferences against the regexp match and replacing the whole line with that expansion, matching real Ansible. Live-reverified with a minimal repro of the exact regexp/line/existing-value combination: `concurrent = 5` (not `concurrent = 5 1`), idempotent on rerun. |
