Live-reverified: `ok=8 changed=0 failed=1 skipped=7`, matching real Ansible's baseline exactly (both engines correctly fail identically on the role's own `network-scripts`/`dhcp-client` packages, which don't exist in Rocky 9's repos - not an engine bug). Earlier: ⚠️ Small regression, not yet root-caused (round 65211/65510, reproduced identically twice): both engines failed, but with an off-by-one gap (`ok=7 skipped=8` krikri vs `ok=8 skipped=7` real Ansible). Earlier still: ✅ Fixed (round 160, 0.9.514). Official RHEL system role. One real bug found and fixed, in `service_facts.cr`: `systemctl list-units`/`list-unit-files` rows are indented with leading whitespace even under `--no-legend` - splitting each line on `/\s+/` WITHOUT stripping first produces a leading empty-string element, shifting every field one column early. The service-name key (parts[0]) landed as `""` instead of the real unit name, and the running/stopped check (parts[3], meant to read the SUB column) actually compared the ACTIVE column instead - so `ansible_facts.services[...]['state']` came back `"stopped"` for EVERY service on the host, including a genuinely-running `NetworkManager.service`. The role's own provider autodetection (`'nm' if 'NetworkManager.service' in ansible_facts.services and ...['state'] == 'running' else 'initscripts'`) always fell back to `'initscripts'` as a result, then tried to install `network-scripts`/`dhcp-client` - packages that don't exist in Rocky 9's repos at all (`Error: Unable to find a match: network-scripts`), where real Ansible correctly autodetects `nm` and skips that whole branch. Fixed by stripping each line before splitting; parsing logic extracted to a new `PluginHelpers::ServiceFactsParser` module for unit testability. Live-reverified locally against the real host's own `systemctl` output: `ansible_facts.services['NetworkManager.service']['state']` now correctly reads `"running"`. |

| kyl191.openvpn | ✅ Not a regression - root-caused (round 65134/65308, 0.9.831 investigation): the single differing task is real Ansible's own `package_facts:` module failing outright ("Could not detect a supported package manager... or the required Python library is not installed") because `python3-apt` isn't installed on this Kata image - `ignore_errors: true` lets the play continue, but the role's own fallback task then runs on real Ansible and is skipped on krikri, whose native (non-Python) `package_facts:` has no such dependency and just succeeds. Not fixable in any meaningful sense - would mean deliberately breaking krikri to match a missing runtime dependency on real Ansible's own side. Earlier: ⚠️ Small regression, not yet root-caused (round 65134/65308, reproduced identically twice): `ok=20/19 changed=14` on krikri vs real Ansible's `ok=21/19`. Earlier still: ✅ Fixed (round 160, 0.9.514). One real bug found and fixed, in `role_loader.cr`: the role's own `defaults/main/openvpn.yml` (a `defaults/main/` DIRECTORY of files - a real, documented Ansible convention, the same one `tasks/main/` already supports - with no single `defaults/main.yml` at all) was never read; `load_vars_file` only ever looked for exactly `defaults/main.yml`, silently returning an empty hash for a role using the directory form. Every one of the role's own defaults (`openvpn_server_network: 10.9.0.0`, `openvpn_server_ipv6_network: "fdbf:..."`) came back undefined as a result, tripping the role's own "Fail if both IPv4 and IPv6 tunnel networks are disabled" validation check that real Ansible never reaches (both are non-empty by default). Fixed by adding `RoleLoader.load_vars_file_main`, which loads either form (single file or `main/` directory, merged alphabetically) and is now used for both `defaults/` and `vars/`. Live-reverified locally with a minimal `defaults/main/*.yml` role: both files' keys now resolve correctly. |

| robertdebock.cve_2024_3094 | ✅ Fixed (round 161, 0.9.515). One real bug found and fixed, in `conditional_evaluator.cr`: `changed_when:`/`failed_when:` never enforced real Ansible's strict-boolean requirement for these two keywords specifically (unlike `when:`, which correctly truthy-converts). The role's own `failed_when: xz_version.stdout | ansible.builtin.regex_search("5\.6\.(0|1)")` against an unaffected xz 5.2.5 (no match, `regex_search` returns `None`) - real Ansible raises "Conditional result (...) was derived from value of type 'NoneType'. Conditionals must have a boolean result." and fails the task; crystal silently truthy-converted `None` to `false` and passed. Fixed via a new `strict:` parameter threaded through `ConditionalEvaluator.evaluate`/`evaluate_truthiness` (not through `not`, which always yields a real bool in Python/Jinja regardless of operand type), raising a new `ConditionalBooleanError` that `apply_changed_failed_when` catches and converts into a failed result. Live-reverified against the exact reproduced case: now correctly `rc=2`, matching real ansible-playbook's failure message. |

Crystal's own host separately hit `Unable to find a match: gitlab-ce-16.0.3` (the role's pinned default version no longer resolvable from that host's repo mirror state at the time) on both cold and warm. |

| robertdebock.release | ✅ Fixed (round 161, 0.9.516). One real bug found and fixed, in `task_executor/executor.cr`'s `execute_include_vars`: `include_vars:` + `with_first_found:` with no matching candidate and no `skip: true` given (the default) previously always SKIPPED the task; real Ansible's `first_found` lookup plugin defaults `skip:` to false and RAISES ("The lookup plugin 'first_found' failed: No file was found when using first_found."), failing the task. The role's own `load release_packages` step (no `CentOS-9.yml`/`Rocky-9.yml` vars file exists in the role at all) - real ansible-playbook correctly fails there; crystal previously silently skipped past it and continued, never loading `release_packages` at all. Fixed by consulting the already-parsed (but previously unused at this call site) `task.loop_first_found_skip` flag. `resolve_first_found`'s general with_first_found loop path (used by modules other than `include_vars:`) still has the old skip-regardless behavior - documented in the code, not reproduced live, not fixed this round. Live-reverified with a minimal repro role: now correctly fails with the exact real-Ansible message. |

| robertdebock.bios_update | ✅ Re-fixed (round 64000, 0.9.815). Found via the 200-role regression round: `ansible_product_version` (a DMI fact) was never gathered at all - the role's own `rescue:` block references it in a `debug: msg`, which real Ansible resolves fine but this engine raised "'ansible_product_version' is undefined" (ironic, since that's exactly the strict-undefined behavior the round-161 fix below added on purpose, just tripped by a fact gap rather than a genuine role bug). Fixed by reading `/sys/class/dmi/id/product_version`, same "NA" fallback as the existing `ansible_system_vendor` DMI fact. Confirm round (0.9.817): byte-identical `ok=9 changed=4 failed=0` cold, `ok=9 changed=1` warm, both engines. Earlier: ✅ Fixed (round 161, 0.9.517). Found a real, pervasive architectural gap: real Ansible uses Jinja2 strict-undefined for module-arg rendering (including inside `rescue:` blocks) and raises on an undefined variable; this engine rendered undefined lookups as the literal string `"undefined"` and continued. The role's own `rescue:` block references `bios_update_download_bios_update_bootable_cd`, genuinely never set on this failure path - real Ansible failed a second time trying to render that rescue's own `debug: msg:` and the whole play ended `failed`; crystal previously rendered `"...Error: undefined"` and reported success. Fixed in `variable_substitutor.cr`: a new `strict:` parameter on `VarSubstitutor#substitute`, used only by `#substitute_task_params` (module-arg/param finalization), raises a new `UndefinedVariableError` for a BARE variable reference (`foo`/`foo.bar`/`foo['bar']` - no filters/operators/function calls) that resolves to nothing - caught and converted to a clean failed-task result matching real Ansible's own "Finalization of task args ... failed" message. Deliberately narrow (see `KNOWN_MISSING.md`'s updated "Real gaps" entry for what's still NOT covered: expressions using filters/operators, `when:`, and Crinja block-tag rendering all stay lenient by design). Fixing this also surfaced and fixed a related pre-existing gap it depended on: `shell:`/`command:`'s check-mode-skip result was missing `stdout`/`stdout_lines`/`stderr`/`stderr_lines`/`rc`/etc. entirely (just `changed`/`failed`/`msg`) - real Ansible's own check-mode skip populates the full normal result shape with empty/zero/null values instead, verified live against ansible-core 2.19.4; a `register:`-ed reference to e.g. `result.stdout` after a check-mode-skipped `shell:` task previously rendered "undefined" silently, now correctly resolves to `""`. Live-reverified against the exact repro: both engines now fail identically at the same task with the same message. `crystal spec`: 1573 examples, 0 failures. |

| robertdebock.atom | ⚠️ Same-fail on both engines (re-verified round 173, Rocky 9.6) - byte-identical `ok=3 changed=1 failed=1 skipped=2` recap on both engines. Confirmed not an engine bug; round 162's "plausibly external repo/download slowness" reading stands. |
| robertdebock.awx | ✅ Fixed (round 162, 0.9.518). Real Ansible removed the `ansible.builtin.include:` action plugin entirely after 2023-05-16 - real ansible-playbook refuses to even START the run when a playbook uses it (`rc=1`, zero tasks execute); this engine treated it as merely "Plugin not available: include" (the same soft per-task skip as any not-yet-implemented module) and kept executing every task after it - one of those later tasks then failed for a completely unrelated real reason (insufficient host resources), masking that the whole run should never have started. Fixed via a new `RemovedActionError` in `playbook_parser.cr` that bypasses the normal per-task/per-play graceful-degradation rescues and propagates to the top-level "Error parsing playbook:" handler, matching real Ansible's `rc=1`/nothing-executes behavior. Live-reverified: byte-identical error message against real ansible-playbook 2.19.4. |

| robertdebock.openbao_agent | ✅ Fixed (round 162, 0.9.519). Real Ansible's `file:` module raises "chown failed: failed to look up user/group `<name>`" and fails the task when `owner:`/`group:` names a system user/group that doesn't exist - it never attempts the chown syscall. This engine's `file.cr` instead left the resolved uid/gid at their "not set" sentinel when the user/group lookup came back empty, then silently skipped the chown call entirely (its own `if uid != -1 \|\| gid != -1` guard) and reported success - the role's own "Ensure OpenBao agent configuration directory exists" task (`owner: openbao`, before any earlier task creates that system user) correctly fails in real Ansible; crystal silently created the directory as `root:root` and continued running every later task in the role (service actually started, config `changed=4`). Fixed: `apply_single_file_attributes` now raises immediately on an owner/group lookup miss, matching real Ansible's exact message, threaded past two pre-existing blanket rescues that would otherwise have swallowed it. Live-reverified: byte-identical failure message against real ansible-playbook 2.19.4. |

Not an engine bug. |

The apache role's own "Remove default vhost in sites-enabled." task references it directly (`when: not ansible_check_mode`) and hard-failed ("'ansible_check_mode' is undefined") under 0.9.517's strict module-arg templating. Added the env var to `run_bench.sh`; byte-identical `rc=0` cold and warm on both engines on re-run. **This also retroactively corrects round163's `geerlingguy.jenkins` conclusion** (previously written off as "external role/upstream drift") - almost certainly the exact same missing env var, not verified further. |

| buluma.confluence | ⚠️ Small regression, single data point (round 65500, 0.9.824 at commit 79270d9a - the original round-65140 attempt and one retry both hit SSH_TIMEOUT before this): `ok=27 changed=8 skipped=6` real Ansible vs `ok=26 changed=7 skipped=7` krikri (cold), similar warm - close to but not exactly the historical `ok=25` baseline below. Not yet root-caused; only confirmed once, unlike the other regressions in this batch. Earlier: ✅ Fixed (round 165, 0.9.523-0.9.526). Found and fixed THREE real, compounding bugs in the same live re-test: (1) `query('first_found', ...)` as an `include_vars:` loop source was entirely broken (`query()` unrecognized as a function, `parse_include_vars_task` never handled a templated `loop:`, `loop_control:` never parsed for `include_vars:` tasks) - 0.9.523; (2) a `loop:` item requiring TWO levels of variable indirection (the common `release -> version -> download-dict` idiom) lost its native Hash type in a 2+-element loop, so `item.checksum` reported "undefined" against a value that was never actually a Hash - extensively bisected live (confirmed NOT batching-specific, NOT `become:`-related, NOT local-vs-remote-specific - a general engine gap) - 0.9.525; (3) `template:`/`copy:` `src:` doubled the subdir when a role baked the prefix into `src:` itself (`src: "./templates/opt/.../file.j2"`, real Ansible resolves this against the role ROOT) - 0.9.526. After all three fixes, the WHOLE role runs to completion (`ok=25 failed=0`); the Confluence service itself still fails to start, a real resource limitation (Java app needing more RAM than a `G3.2GB` test VM has), not a krikri-playbook bug. `crystal spec`: 1590 examples, 0 failures. |

| buluma.maven | ✅ Fixed (round 165, 0.9.524). Real Ansible's `file:` module accepts `owner:`/`group:` as a raw numeric uid/gid string, not just a name - `group: "0"` (the role's own "Create Maven installation directory" task) always raised "chown failed: failed to look up group 0" after round162's 0.9.519 strict-lookup fix, which only ever tried a name lookup. Fixed in two places: the strict-lookup-failure path now falls back to treating an all-digits string as a raw id, and the SEPARATE idempotency-comparison path now compares a numeric owner/group against the raw uid/gid directly instead of a resolved name (an already-correct numeric group previously never matched its resolved NAME, reporting `changed: true` forever). Live-reverified; byte-identical `rc=0` cold and warm on both engines after the fix. |

Live-verified against real Rocky 9.6: crystal now reaches the identical real failure real ansible-playbook hits (package `atlassian-bitbucket-100:10.2.4*` genuinely unavailable in the configured repo - an external/role issue, not a crystal bug, once the include itself runs). |

| buluma.elasticsearch_curator | 🐛 Real bug found and fixed (round 166, 0.9.527) - `ansible.builtin.yum`/`dnf` with `enablerepo: epel` on a host with no EPEL repo configured hard-failed ("Error: Unknown repo: 'epel'") in crystal's plugin, which shells out to the real `dnf`/`yum` CLI (strict). Real ansible's module goes through dnf's Python API, which just warns and continues using whatever repos ARE configured. Fixed via a `remote_exec_tolerating_unknown_repo` retry helper in both `plugins/dnf.cr` and `plugins/yum.cr` that strips the offending `--enablerepo=X` flag and retries. Live-verified against real Rocky 9.6 (`pip` installed successfully; full role now converges with real ansible-playbook's `ok=7 failed=0`, idempotent on rerun). |

| buluma.gitlab_ce | 🐛 2 real bugs found and fixed (round 167, 0.9.528/0.9.529, Ubuntu 22.04). (1) `ansible.builtin.apt_key`'s idempotency check (`key_present?`) ran `apt-key --keyring X list` against a keyring file X that didn't exist yet - an undocumented side effect of that command is creating X as an EMPTY file in GnuPG's modern "keybox" format, not the classic OpenPGP binary format apt's own `trusted.gpg.d` reader requires. The later `apt-key --keyring X add` (in `#add_key`) then found X already existing in keybox format and inherited it, producing a keyring apt rejected outright ("the key(s) ... are ignored as the file has an unsupported filetype"). Fixed by skipping the `list` call entirely when the keyring file doesn't exist yet (a nonexistent keyring trivially can't contain the key) - `plugins/apt_key.cr`. (2) Also gave `key_fingerprints`'s own `gpg --import --show-only` call an isolated `--homedir` so it can't touch/poison the shared `~/.gnupg` state either. Both fixed and live-verified against the real Rocky/Ubuntu host: both keys write correct classic-format keyrings, `apt-get update` runs clean with zero "unsupported filetype" warnings, reruns are idempotent. |

| buluma.zeppelin | 🐛 Real bug found and fixed (round 167, 0.9.529, Ubuntu 22.04) - `ansible.builtin.user` with `groups:` (not `group:`) set on a brand-new user, where a group of the SAME NAME as the user already exists (this role's own prior `group: {name: zeppelin}` task), needs `useradd`'s `-N` (no-user-group) flag to stop `useradd`'s own default private-group-creation from colliding with that already-existing group - matches real `ansible.builtin.user`'s own `create_user_useradd` source logic exactly. Without it: `useradd: group zeppelin exists - if you want to add this user to that group, use -g.` Fixed in `plugins/user.cr` (`group_exists?` helper + `-N` insertion in `#create`). Live-verified against a real Ubuntu 22.04 host: user creation now succeeds, idempotent on rerun. |

Fixed generically in `task_executor/executor.cr`'s `base_context_a_for` - synthesizes `ansible_ssh_user`/`ansible_ssh_host`/`ansible_ssh_port` as bidirectional aliases of `ansible_user`/`ansible_host`/`ansible_port`. Live-verified. |

| geerlingguy.php_versions | 🐛 Real bug found and fixed (round 168, 0.9.531) - `include_vars: "{{ item }}"` paired with `with_fileglob:` (not `with_first_found:`/`loop:`) was never recognized by the dedicated `include_vars:` parser (`parse_include_vars_task`), unlike the generic task parser which already handles `with_fileglob:` for every other module. `item` stayed completely unbound, rendering the literal text "undefined" and failing with "include_vars: file not found: undefined" instead of globbing the role's own `vars/` directory the way real Ansible does. Fixed in `playbook_parser.cr` (`parse_include_vars_task` now sets `task.loop_fileglob`) and `task_executor/executor.cr` (`execute_include_vars`'s loop-resolution fallback chain now also tries `resolve_fileglob`). Live-verified with a real glob pattern matching a real vars file. |

| geerlingguy.ssh_chroot_jail | 🐛 Real bug found and fixed (round 168, 0.9.532) - a LOOPED handler's own `when:` (the "add binary libs via l2chroot" handler: `when: item.l2chroot is not defined or item.l2chroot`, `with_items: "{{ ssh_chroot_bins }}"`) was evaluated ONCE, before the loop even resolved - with no `item` bound at all, `item.l2chroot is not defined` was trivially true regardless of any individual item's real value, so EVERY item ran unconditionally, including `/usr/bin/which` (whose own `l2chroot: false` should have skipped it) - failing since `which` isn't a dynamic executable ("not a dynamic executable... /bin/cp: missing destination file operand"). Fixed in `task_executor/executor.cr`: moved the `when:` check out of `execute_handler_internal` (pre-loop) and into `execute_handler_plugin_once` (which runs once per loop iteration with `item` properly bound, and once with no `item` for a non-looped handler - covering both cases identically). Live-verified: the `which` item now correctly skips. This role's own `cr warm` result already reflects the in-flight fix (rebuilt mid-role, matching prior rounds' practice for a fix found and shipped during the same round it was found in). |

| robertdebock.cloud9 | ⚠️ Not a new bug (round 169, Rocky 9.6) - a combination of 3 factors, none of them an engine divergence: (1) this round's own test setup never installed the role's `robertdebock.service` dependency, so real ansible-playbook fails at PARSE time with an unrelated "role not found" error before it ever reaches the task krikri-playbook failed on; (2) the role itself doesn't support Rocky/RHEL at all - its own `_cloud9_requirements` dict has no `Rocky`/`RedHat`/`CentOS`-family entry AND no `default` fallback key, so `cloud9_requirements` genuinely can't be computed on this OS; (3) that unresolvable value hits the ALREADY-DOCUMENTED `KNOWN_MISSING.md` gap ("a genuinely undefined variable reached through a filter chain... is still an open gap") - it lenient-resolves to the literal string `"undefined"` instead of raising a strict error, which then gets passed to `package: name: "undefined"` and fails with a confusing `dnf` error instead of a clear "role doesn't support this OS" message. Verified via a minimal local repro reproducing the exact filter-chain-leniency mechanism. Not a new bug - documented gap + role/OS mismatch + test-setup gap, not fixed. |

| buluma.auditd | ✅ Fixed (round 170, 0.9.534). Found via cold-run divergence on `assert.yml`'s `(auditd_admin_space_left | int < auditd_space_left | int) if (auditd_space_left | string is not match(".*%")) else true` - `conditional_evaluator.cr`'s `ConditionalEvaluator.evaluate` had NO handling at all for a Python/Jinja ternary (`X if COND else Y`); even the trivial `true if true else false` was broken (an embedded `<`/`==` inside the true-branch got misparsed as a top-level comparison spanning the whole ternary string). Fixed by detecting a top-level, paren/quote-depth-aware `if`/`else` split before the or/and/comparison checks (ternary is the lowest-precedence Python/Jinja construct) and delegating the whole condition to Crinja. Live-reverified: byte-identical `ok=44 changed=6 skipped=5` cold, idempotent warm rerun, after the fix. |

| buluma.bind | ✅ Regression root-caused and fixed (round 65144/65310 found it, live-reverified on a dedicated Atlantic Ubuntu 22.04 host, 0.9.827): `bind9.service` is a systemd `Alias=` of `named.service`, and `systemctl enable bind9` genuinely refuses on Ubuntu 22.04 ("Refusing to operate on alias name or linked unit file") - but real Ansible's own `systemd` module runs `is-enabled '<name>' -l`, whose multi-line output for an alias never string-matches its own `"alias"` exclusion check, so it accidentally never calls `enable` at all and reports success. `plugins/systemd.cr`'s own `enabled?` used single-line `is-enabled` with a strict `== "enabled"` check, correctly saw "alias", and (reasonably by itself) tried to enable anyway - hitting the refusal real Ansible's own quirk happens to dodge. Fixed by replicating real Ansible's exact `-l` + string-comparison logic (`SystemdEnabledState`, `src/krikri/plugin_helpers/systemd_enabled_state.cr`). Regression spec added (`spec/unit/systemd_enabled_state_spec.cr`). Earlier: ⚠️ Regression, not yet root-caused (round 65144/65310, reproduced identically twice): krikri failed outright (`rc=2 failed=1`) where real Ansible succeeded (`rc=0`). Earlier still: ✅ Fixed (round 170, 0.9.535). Found via cold-run divergence: `vars/Debian.yml`'s `(bind_python_version == '3') | ternary('python3-netaddr', 'python-netaddr')` always picked the FALSE branch on crystal, installing the removed python2-era `python-netaddr`/`python-dnspython` package names instead of `python3-*`. Root cause: `crinja_renderer.cr`'s `rerender_nested_templates` blindly `JSON.parse`-back'd EVERY re-rendered nested-template scalar (not just container-shaped array/dict results), so a purely numeric-looking STRING default (`bind_python_version: "{{ bind_default_python_version }}"`, referencing the quoted string `"3"`) silently became the integer `3`, breaking the `== '3'` string-literal comparison downstream (real Ansible preserves the string type through `{{ var }}`-only indirections). Fixed by only parsing back when the rendered text is container-shaped (`[`/`{`-prefixed); otherwise kept as a string. Live-reverified clean after the fix. |

| buluma.collectd | ✅ Fixed (round 170 follow-up, 0.9.541, vendored Crinja fork `crystal-play-0.9.17`). The original divergence (the pre-existing, previously-documented Crinja explicit-dash whitespace-control gap, round85 precedent) and a second issue found while verifying it (a trailing `{{ collectd_conf_extra }}`, default `null`, rendering as the literal `none` instead of empty) are BOTH fixed now, and both traced to real root causes rather than patched around: (1) `Token#reset` never reset `trim_left`/`trim_right`, so a reused lexer token carried a stale dash flag from an EARLIER tag onto a LATER plain one with no dash at all - fixed in the Crinja fork itself (`Token#reset`), not a workaround in krikri-playbook; (2) `None` finalized inconsistently depending on which fork of this same fix I first tried - the real behavior (verified against real `ansible-playbook`) is that Ansible's own Jinja `finalize` hook converts `None` to `""` ONLY at a template's own top-level `{{ }}` output, while the SAME `None` flowing through a filter (`join`, etc.) still renders as Python's real `"None"` - fixed at the correct narrow layer (`Renderer#render`'s own `PrintStatement` visit), not the shared `Finalizer`. Live-reverified: rendering `collectd.conf.j2` with real Ansible vs krikri-playbook now produces byte-identical output including the trailing blank line, no `none` text anywhere. Crinja's own full spec suite (550 examples) and krikri-playbook's own (1613 examples) both pass clean. Not re-verified against a real host this round (verified via local template-render diff only) - worth a dedicated live re-check on a future round. |

| robertdebock.haproxy | ✅ Fixed (round 171, 0.9.542). Found a real recap divergence: the role's "Modify selinux settings" block uses `community.general.seport` (no plugin here) gated by `when: ansible_facts['selinux']['status'] == "enabled"` (false on this host) - real Ansible evaluates `when:` before resolving the module and prints "skipping"; this engine dropped the whole task at PARSE time (before `when:` was ever evaluated), so it vanished from the recap entirely (`skipped=18` vs real's `19`). Fixed two ways: added a real `seport` plugin (`semanage port` SELinux port-type mapping), and fixed the general bug (`when_passes?` now always skips an unavailable-module task regardless of its own `when:`, closing the same class of divergence for every currently- or future-unimplemented module). Live-reverified clean. |

| robertdebock.revealmd | ✅ Fixed (round 171, 0.9.545). Two issues, one harness gap and one real bug. Harness gap: the role's `Create revealmd service` task does `import_role: name: robertdebock.service` (itself depending on `robertdebock.npm`), neither installed in `requirements.yml` - real Ansible failed immediately with "role not found" on both; installed the missing roles. Real bug found on the clean re-run: `ok=17` (real) vs `ok=18` (crystal) - `import_role:` is resolved statically by real Ansible (no task result of its own, a true parse-time splice), but this engine reused `include_role:`'s dynamic runtime machinery, which shows its own "TASK [...]" banner and counts its own `ok` - correct for `include_role:` but wrong for `import_role:`. Fixed via a new `Task#is_static_import` flag threaded through the executor's banner-printing and `ok`/`skipped` counting (3 call sites: the top-level batch loop, the nested block/rescue/always list, and the block-skip printer) so a static import produces no result of its own, matching real Ansible, while the included role's own tasks still get their normal banners either way. Live-reverified on a fresh throwaway pair after both fixes: byte-identical `ok=17 changed=7 skipped=8` cold, idempotent warm rerun, on both engines. `crystal spec`: 1613 examples, 0 failures. |

Cold diverged: real Ansible's `Systemctl daemon-reload` handler (`daemon-reload: true`, the real, documented hyphenated alias per `ansible-doc ansible.builtin.systemd`) succeeded; this engine's `systemd.cr` only read the canonical `daemon_reload` name and failed the handler with "Must specify at least one of...". Warm masked it (nothing changed, handler never notified). Fixed by falling back to each documented alias (`daemon-reload`/`daemon-reexec`/`service`/`unit`). Live-reverified clean on a fresh throwaway pair after the fix: byte-identical cold+warm, idempotent, on both engines. |

| buluma.rpmfusion | ✅ Fixed (round 172, 0.9.546). Found a real recap divergence: `ok=8` matched on both, but `changed=4` (real) vs `changed=5` (crystal) - the role's own "Yum update cache" handler (`ansible.builtin.dnf: {update_cache: true}`, no `name:`) reported `changed:` on crystal, `ok:` (unchanged) on real Ansible, which never reports changed for a pure dnf/yum cache refresh (no reliable "was it actually stale" signal). `dnf.cr`/`yum.cr`'s own separate cache-only branches (reached only via a direct `dnf:`/`yum:` call, not the generic `package:`) had the old `changed: result[:exit_code] == 0` logic; `package.cr`'s identical path already had this right from an earlier round. Live-reverified clean on a fresh throwaway pair after the fix: byte-identical `ok=8 changed=4 skipped=0` cold, idempotent warm rerun, on both engines. |

| buluma.sosreport | ✅ Fixed (round 172, 0.9.547). Found a real crash: crystal's `Fetch sosreports` task (`ansible.builtin.fetch:`, under play-level `become: true`, even though the SSH connection was already root) crashed with "Failed to parse plugin output" / "sudo: a password is required", while real Ansible succeeded cleanly - fetch always runs on the controller, and `execute_local_plugin`'s generic become-wrapping (`sudo -n -u <user> --` around the WHOLE plugin process) doesn't distinguish a controller-only plugin, which real Ansible's fetch never needs local privilege escalation for at all. Fixed by skipping the sudo wrap for any `controller_only?` plugin regardless of the task's own `become:`. Live-reverified clean on a fresh throwaway pair after the fix: byte-identical cold+warm, idempotent, on both engines. |

| mrlesmithjr.lighttpd | ✅ Fixed and verified (round 180, 0.9.592). Cold run initially diverged on the role's `debian | Installing Pre-Reqs` apt task (`until: result is successful`): real ansible reported `changed` (genuinely installed a package), krikri-playbook reported `ok`/"already installed" - root cause was krikri-playbook's `until:`/`is successful` Jinja test being entirely unrecognized by both expression evaluators, so the retry loop always ran its full default 3 attempts regardless of the first attempt's real success; by the 3rd (now-idempotent) attempt the package was already installed, and that attempt's `changed: false` result is what got registered. Fixed by adding `successful`/`failure`/`change`/`skip` test-name aliases to both `ConditionalEvaluator` and the Crinja test registrations (matching real Ansible's `TestModule.tests`), ordered so a longer alias (`successful`) is checked before any name that's merely its own prefix (`success`) - the same substring-prefix trap already documented for `link_exists`/`link`. Live-reverified clean on the same throwaway host: cold now correctly reports `changed`, warm rerun correctly reports `ok`. Also matches the role's own cold-fails/warm-succeeds non-idempotency shape identically on both engines pre-fix (`rc=2` cold / `rc=0` warm), unrelated to the apt bug. |

Re-verified clean after the fix: cold `changed=4` matches real ansible exactly, warm rerun `changed=0` idempotent on both. |

| mrlesmithjr.nagios | ⚠️ Same-fail on both engines (round 180, Ubuntu 22.04) - identical `rc=1`, legacy `include:` refusal. |
| mrlesmithjr.netdata | ✅ Re-fixed (round 64003, 0.9.816). Found via the 200-role regression round: same `changed_when:`/`stderr_lines` ordering bug as `buluma.netdata` above - the role's own dependency-install task's `changed_when` raised "object of type 'dict' has no attribute 'stderr_lines'". Confirm round (0.9.817): byte-identical `ok=17 changed=6 failed=0 skipped=9` cold, `ok=15 changed=0` warm, both engines. Earlier: ✅ Fixed and verified (round 180, 0.9.593). Cold run initially diverged: real ansible rendered the role's 5934-line `netdata.conf.j2` fine (`rc=0`), krikri-playbook crashed the whole task with "Regex match error: JIT stack limit reached" - two template-rewrite regexes (`INLINE_TERNARY`, and the actual culprit, `JOIN_METHOD`) had lazy/negated-class quantifiers that could cross newlines, and on this huge file (only 26 `{{` and exactly one stray, unmatched `"` in the whole file) each candidate scanned/backtracked across nearly the rest of the file with nothing to match, overflowing PCRE2's JIT match-time stack. Fixed by excluding `\n` from both patterns' character classes. Re-verified live (local `ansible_connection=local` repro, no cloud host needed - template rendering is controller-side): renders cleanly now. |

| buluma.dotfiles | ✅ Fixed and verified (round 180, 0.9.594). Cold run diverged: real ansible succeeded (`rc=0`), krikri-playbook failed (`rc=2`) - the role's own "Remove existing dotfiles file" task gates on `when: "'@' not in item.1.stdout"` over `with_indexed_items:`, and `VariableLookup#resolve_nested`'s dotted-path resolver only handled Hash key lookup for a path segment - a numeric segment (`item.1`, the idiomatic way to pull the second element out of a `with_indexed_items`/`zip()` pair) against an Array fell through to a bare `return nil`, raising "'item.1.stdout' is undefined" instead of resolving the pair. Fixed by adding Array numeric-index support. Re-verified live (local repro). |
| buluma.fish | ✅ Fixed and verified (round 180, 0.9.595). Cold run diverged: real ansible succeeded (`rc=0`), krikri-playbook failed (`rc=2`) - `ansible_facts['lsb']` (the LSB fact block used by the role's Ubuntu PPA apt-repo template, `{{ ansible_facts['lsb'].codename | lower }}`) was entirely unimplemented, so the codename rendered empty, writing a malformed `sources.list` entry that crashed `apt-get update` ("Malformed entry 1 ... (Component)"). Fixed by adding the `ansible_lsb` fact (parses `/etc/lsb-release`). Re-verified live (local repro). |

Fixed alongside `buluma.dotfiles` above. Re-verified live (local repro). Note: substituted for dead-repo `buluma.helm`. |

| buluma.phpmyadmin | ✅ Clean (round 181 re-verification of the round-180 gap, now fixed in `0.9.599`). The round-180 shape - a role default computed from a never-set variable rendering as the literal text `undefined` - is fixed: strict module-arg templating now follows the chain and fails naming the innermost missing variable, matching real Ansible. Re-running the role live surfaced a second, pre-existing bug underneath (also fixed in `0.9.599`): a meta/main.yml DEPENDENCY's defaults were discarded after the dependency's own tasks, so this role's `phpmyadmin_mysql_password: "{{ mysql_root_password }}"` could not see `buluma.mysql`'s own `mysql_root_password` default and wrote the literal `undefined` into `/etc/phpmyadmin/config.inc.php` as the MySQL password. Note the current Galaxy version of this role no longer reproduces round 180's original shape (its `buluma.mysql` dependency now DEFINES `mysql_root_password`), and its `tasks/main.yml` uses the removed `include:` directive, which both engines now refuse identically - the round-181 live run patched that to `include_tasks:` on both sides to get past it. This role also aborts on its own `notify: restart apache` (no such handler exists anywhere in it) - since `0.9.600` both engines abort identically at the same task (`Ensure phpMyAdmin is installed.`, task 162 on both sides) with the same message and rc=1, so the role cannot be run to completion by either Round 183 follow-up: this role's warm-rerun churn is ROLE-SIDE, not an engine bug - real ansible rewrites `php.ini` on every warm run too, because `geerlingguy.php` and `buluma.php` both own that file. The comparison surfaced a real scoping divergence (cross-role vars/defaults visibility), FIXED in `0.9.603`. The `Configure httpd` difference chased alongside it was an artifact of alternating both engines against one shared host; on a clean single-engine sequence both alternate `ok` then `changed` identically, since httpd's template strips the `Include /etc/phpmyadmin/apache.conf` line phpmyadmin's `lineinfile` re-adds |

| buluma.revealmd | ✅ Fixed and verified (round 180, 0.9.597). Cold run initially diverged: real ansible refused the whole playbook immediately (`rc=1`, zero tasks run - "the role 'buluma.service' was not found", from the role's own task-level `import_role: name: buluma.service`, a role never installed), while krikri-playbook ran 5 of revealmd's own tasks before failing (`rc=2`) at the runtime `_include_role` dispatch instead. Root cause: this engine's own task-level `import_role:` had no existence check at all for a literal (non-templated) role name - only a play-level `roles:` entry or a role's own `meta/main.yml` dependency were checked (0.9.590). Fixed by adding `RoleLoader.role_exists?` (a public wrapper around the existing role search) at the same parse-time call site, matching real Ansible's static resolution. Re-verified live: `rc=1`, zero tasks run, identical message shape. |
| buluma.vagrant | ✅ Fixed and verified (round 180, 0.9.598). Cold run initially diverged: real ansible failed (`rc=2`, "Unable to find any of pip3 to use.  pip needs to be installed." - no pip3 on this fresh host at all), krikri-playbook succeeded (`rc=0`) - the role's own `vagrant_pip_packages` default is an empty list `[]` on Debian-family, and this engine's `pip:` module checked "is there anything to install" (no) before ever checking whether the pip executable exists, silently no-opping instead of matching real Ansible's `get_bin_path`-style up-front existence check (which fails regardless of whether there's anything to install). Fixed by adding a `which`-based existence check before the empty-name no-op branch. Re-verified live: `rc=2`, matching real ansible's exact failure. |

| andrewrothstein.bazelisk | ⚠️ Round 186 (Ubuntu 22.04): found a real bug - its `andrewrothstein.temurin` dependency builds a download filename from a multi-line YAML `|-` block scalar, one `{{ part -}}` span per line, relying on the `-}}` trim marker to collapse the block's own line breaks - the marker CHARACTER was stripped but its whitespace-trimming EFFECT was never applied, so every line break survived into the literal filename/URL, breaking the download outright (400 Bad Request). Fixed in 0.9.614, both cold/warm now match real Ansible exactly (real 9.9s/9.2s vs crystal cold/warm rc=0/0 post-fix). |

| buluma.p10k | ⚠️ Round 186 (Ubuntu 22.04): found a real bug - `when: zsh_version.stdout | float < 5.1` - a bare FLOAT literal ("5.1") on the right side of a comparison had no case in `#evaluate_value` (only bare INTs did), so it fell through to plain variable lookup, found no var literally named "5.1", and raised "'5.1' is undefined" under strict-undefined `when:` checking; fixing it also exposed `#compare_values` had no float-numeric fallback either (only int), which would have compared "10.2" < "5.1" lexicographically-wrong once the literal was recognized. Fixed in 0.9.615, both cold/warm now match real Ansible exactly (real 16.4s/16.1s vs crystal cold/warm rc=0/0 post-fix). |

| konstruktoid.docker_rootless | ⚠️ Round 187 (Rocky 9.6): found a real bug - three stacked bugs: `systemd_service`'s `scope: user` was completely unhandled (every systemctl call always hit the SYSTEM manager); fixing that exposed real Ansible's own auto-set `XDG_RUNTIME_DIR` for scope:user with no equivalent here; fixing THAT exposed the real root cause - block-level `become:`/`become_user:` was never inherited by child tasks at all, so the whole block silently ran as root instead of the intended user. | ipr-cnrs.glpi_agent | ✅ Fixed (0.9.741). Original divergence: `lookup('first_found', params)` (no `paths:`) inside `include_tasks:` resolved against `files/templates/vars/.` in that order, missing the role's own `tasks/` dir entirely - matched `vars/Debian.yml` (a same-named file) instead of `tasks/Debian.yml`, then failed trying to run it as a tasks list. Real Ansible's own `DataLoader#path_dwim_relative_stack` searches `files/` then the raw `tasks/` dir when no `paths:` given; fixed by inserting `tasks` into the default search order right after `files`. Live-reverified (podman/Debian 12, ansible-core 2.14.18): `ok=6 changed=1 failed=1 skipped=0` on both engines now, exact match - both fail identically at a later task on `glpi-agent` genuinely not being available in this repo (environmental, not an engine bug). No formal cold/warm timing captured for this confirmation pass (ad-hoc podman re-verification, not a full benchmark round). |

| itigoag.packages | ✅ Fixed (round 301/302, 0.9.736-0.9.737). Original divergence was `json_query` entirely unimplemented; fixed via a real JMESPath engine (`src/krikri/jmespath.cr`, 0.9.736). Live-reverified (podman/Debian 12, round 302, 0.9.737): `json_query` output matches real Ansible exactly on matching/non-matching queries. Also surfaced an unrelated real bug the role's own `with_dict: "{{ packages }}"` depends on - `package_facts:`'s `ansible_facts.packages` was silently clobbering a play-level `vars: packages:` of the same bare name (facts-vs-play-vars precedence); fixed 0.9.737, see `KNOWN_MISSING.md`'s round 302 narrative. No formal cold/warm timing captured for this confirmation pass (ad-hoc podman re-verification, not a full benchmark round). |

## Round 6000-6007 (first krikri-role-tester round, 8 new-author roles, 0.9.761)

First round driven by the new `krikri-role-tester` Crystal harness (`../krikri-role-tester`,
replacing the shell drivers) instead of by hand. The round itself surfaced two real bugs in the
new tool, not in krikri-playbook: `Cmd.run` raced `Process#wait` against its own output-reading
fibers (crashed the very first attempt outright), and Atlantic.net teardown silently failed to
pass its required `-var role=`/`-var round=` on destroy - leaking every Atlantic.net pair the tool
had ever torn down normally, confirmed and manually cleaned up. Both fixed with regression specs
before this table's numbers were collected on the re-run.

|---|---|
| fauust.mariadb | ⚠️ Both fail before a real recap forms (round6002, 0.9.761, Kata/Debian trixie). rc cold py=4 cr=4, warm py=4 cr=4. Times: cold py 32.49s vs cr 21.23s; warm py 6.35s vs cr 1.30s. Role needs the `ansible.mariadb` collection (not installed in this harness); real ansible-playbook fatals with no PLAY RECAP at all, krikri completes a full recap (`ok=19 changed=4 skipped=16`) by skipping the unresolvable module - same collection-module scope-cut class as other rounds, not a new engine bug. `ansible.mariadb.*` is aliased onto the implemented community.mysql plugins as of 0.9.825; row needs a confirm-phase re-run before status changes. |
| influxdata.chrony | ⚠️ Both fail identically, no recap either side (round6000, 0.9.761, Kata/Debian trixie). rc cold py=1 cr=1, warm py=1 cr=1 identical. Times: cold py 0.67s vs cr 0.01s; warm py 0.68s vs cr 0.01s. Role's `tasks/main.yml` uses `include:`, removed from ansible-core after 2023-05-16 - real ansible-playbook fatals at parse time identically on both engines. |

## Round 6008-6019 (second krikri-role-tester round / stability check, 8 new-author roles, 0.9.761)

A third real krikri-role-tester bug surfaced here: `Cmd.run` passed its carefully-stripped
`Cmd.engine_env` to `Process.new` without `clear_env: true` - Crystal's `Process.new` MERGES a
given `env:` onto the parent's full environment by default rather than replacing it, so every
engine subprocess kept inheriting this control machine's own ambient `ANSIBLE_CACHE_PLUGIN=
community.general.pickle`/`ANSIBLE_CACHE_PLUGIN_CONNECTION=/tmp/ansible_facts_cache` regardless.
Confirmed live: real ansible-playbook wrote/read a fact-cache entry keyed by the harness's generic
`pyhost` inventory alias, which is identical every round even though the underlying VM is torn
down and rebuilt each time - poisoning `dev-sec.mysql`/`dev-sec.nginx-hardening`/
`galaxyproject.postgresql_objects`/`ansible-lockdown.ubuntu22_cis`'s first real Kata round with a
stale `/usr/bin/python3.10` interpreter path from some earlier, unrelated host. Fixed by passing
`clear_env: true` for engine runs (regression spec added); those 4 roles were then re-run clean
(round6016-6019) after clearing the poisoned cache files. The 4 Atlantic.net roles in the same
batch (round6012-6015) are recorded as originally run - Atlantic.net always provisions a fresh
real IP per pair, so there was no matching evidence of poisoning there, though the same
`pyhost`/`crhost` alias reuse means it isn't ruled out either. No leaked Atlantic.net
infrastructure this round - all 4 terraform destroys verified rc=0 with 0 resources remaining in
state afterward.

| Role | Status |
|---|---|
| ansible-lockdown.ubuntu22_cis | ✅ Clean (round6019, 0.9.761, Kata/Debian trixie, re-run after cache-poisoning fix). rc cold py=2 cr=2, warm py=2 cr=2. Times: cold py 4.52s vs cr 1.39s; warm py 3.77s vs cr 0.52s. |
| bertvv.hosts | ✅ Clean (round6014, 0.9.761, Atlantic/Ubuntu 22.04). rc cold py=0 cr=0, warm py=0 cr=0. Times: cold py 6.75s vs cr 4.64s; warm py 3.77s vs cr 0.52s. |

Off-by-one `changed` on "Update apt cache if MySQL is not yet installed" - krikri reports it changed where py doesn't; not chased further this round. |

healthy services, config parity) as of the last time it was run. ⚠️ = engine
itself is fine, but the role hit an external/environmental blocker partway
through. ❌ = role could not be run at all (Galaxy removal or a bug in the
role itself, not krikri-playbook).
| prometheus.prometheus.pushgateway | ✅ Fixed (round 198, 0.9.638, Ubuntu 22.04). Fixed by giving `build_vars_context` a real `vars` key; verified against ansible-core 2.19.4. |

### Round 199: 60-role marathon, fresh host pair per role (40 ubuntu + 20 rocky), idempotency-verified

First round using CLAUDE.md's revised "fresh host pair per round" guidance (previously a pair was reused for a handful of roles). Also the round that found the bodsch.* author's collection (`bodsch.core`/`bodsch.systemd`) custom-module/filter scope cut applies well beyond the two previously-documented roles - confirmed on 8 more. 2 real krikri-playbook bugs found and fixed (0.9.642, 0.9.643); 1 more found and documented not fixed. Full per-role timing and verdicts below.

| bodsch.chrony | ubuntu | ⚠️ both fail rc=4 for DIFFERENT reasons (role's own wrong `bodsch.core.journalctl` FQCN trips real ansible; crystal skips the role-private `library/chrony.py` custom module per the documented no-arbitrary-Python scope cut, then fails downstream on the resulting undefined var) |

### Round 200: 60-role marathon, fresh host pair per role (40 ubuntu + 20 rocky)

3 real krikri-playbook bugs found and fixed (0.9.644 assert: strict:true, 0.9.645 Crinja Tuple-sort comparison, 0.9.646 ternary loop: source resolving to a Python-repr string instead of a real list). 1 more real bug found and documented but not fixed after an attempted fix caused regressions elsewhere (andrewrothstein.traefik, see KNOWN_MISSING.md). Remaining roles were clean/identical or role-side bugs both engines hit the same way - many mrlesmithjr roles use the ansible-core-removed `include:` action. ### Round 201: first parallel-batch round (4 concurrent pairs, one per role, 8 of the 10-server Atlantic.net account's slots)

First live test of the new parallel batch-phase workflow in CLAUDE.md (up to 4 concurrent host pairs instead of one at a time). 4 of the first 4 role picks (`geerlingguy.rsyslog`, `.gunicorn`, `.tailscale`, `.grafana`) turned out to be Galaxy-404 despite not being in this file's tested list - a reminder that "not in ROLES_TESTED.md" doesn't confirm a role still exists; verify with an exact Galaxy API name filter (`/api/v1/roles/?owner__username=X&name=Y`) before provisioning, not by absence from this table alone. Replaced with 2 Galaxy-verified roles. No engine bugs confirmed and fixed this round - one finding (kubelet idempotency on the kubernetes role) could not be conclusively reproduced in isolation and is documented in KNOWN_MISSING.md instead of fixed blind.

| geerlingguy.rsyslog | ubuntu | ❌ untestable - not on Ansible Galaxy (404), same class as `.mongodb`/`.consul`/`.golang` |
| geerlingguy.gunicorn | ubuntu | ❌ untestable - not on Ansible Galaxy (404) |
| geerlingguy.tailscale | ubuntu | ❌ untestable - not on Ansible Galaxy (404) |
| geerlingguy.grafana | ubuntu | ❌ untestable - not on Ansible Galaxy (404) |
| geerlingguy.kubernetes | ubuntu | ✅ Clean, identical both cold and warm (round 70002, 0.9.835: `ok=9 changed=5 failed=1 skipped=2` cold, `ok=8 changed=0 failed=1 skipped=3` warm, both engines, both failing at the same kubeadm/kernel precondition - environmental). Resolves the round 65166/65311 divergence (`changed=4` vs `changed=5`): root cause was this engine emulating real Ansible's python3-apt auto-install as a per-invocation changed=false rule WITHOUT ever performing the install, so a host starting without the bindings stayed on the absent path forever while real Ansible's host moved to the mtime-diff path after its first apt task (the role's "Ensure dependencies are installed." task); the role's `apt: {update_cache: true}, when: kubernetes_repository.changed` task then fetched the fresh pkgs.k8s.io indexes (genuinely moving the lists mtime) and reported changed for real Ansible but ok for krikri. Fixed in 0.9.835 by implementing the auto-install faithfully (shared `AptLockRetry#apt_auto_install_python_apt` helper, wired into apt.cr's module start and package.cr's cache-refresh-only path - real `package:` delegates to the apt module and auto-installs the same way); round-30001's first-invocation semantics preserved exactly, since the respawned module's mtime window opens entirely after the prefetch in both engines. Regression spec added for the helper. See KNOWN_MISSING.md's 0.9.835 narrative. Earlier fixes: round 201 follow-up (0.9.648, service.cr `activating` state). Times: cold py 42.8s vs cr 31.4s; warm py 13.0s vs cr 1.9s |
| geerlingguy.raspberry-pi | ubuntu | ✅ clean on cold (role targets Raspberry Pi hardware; on generic Ubuntu x86_64 both engines fail identically at "Configure options in /boot/firmware/config.txt", `ok=1 changed=0 failed=1` both). One cosmetic-only difference found: krikri printed the next task's `TASK [...]` banner even on an already-halted host, where real Ansible ends the play once no host remains active - fixed in 0.9.647 (`run_task_batch` now breaks once `active_hosts` is empty, instead of only gating the block/include_tasks branches on it). Warm rerun differs for an unrelated, already-known reason: krikri has no fact-caching (`ANSIBLE_CACHE_PLUGIN_CONNECTION`) support, so it always re-gathers facts where real Ansible (with that env var set) skips "Gathering Facts" on rerun - documented as a real feature gap in KNOWN_MISSING.md. Times: cold py 5.7s vs cr 7.0s; warm py 2.1s vs cr 1.0s |

### Round 300-303: first batch of a 100-role parallel campaign (4 concurrent pairs, roles pre-verified against Galaxy's exact-match API before provisioning)

Candidate roles this time were sourced from real GitHub repo listings for prolific Galaxy authors not yet exhausted in this table (avoiding geerlingguy/robertdebock/linux-system-roles/mrlesmithjr/Oefenweb/andrewrothstein - all heavily covered), then each individually confirmed via `/api/v1/roles/?owner__username=X&name=Y` returning `count: 1` before spending any infrastructure on it - the lesson from round 201's 4-for-4 Galaxy-404 miss. 3 of 4 roles clean; 1 (`cloudalchemy.cortex`) surfaced 3 real bugs, all fixed in 0.9.649.

| cloudalchemy.cortex | ubuntu | ⚠️ 3 real krikri-playbook bugs found and fixed in 0.9.649. (1) `to_nice_yaml`/`to_yaml` rendered an empty dict as the literal `--- {}` instead of `{}` - Crystal's `YAML::Any#to_yaml` emits an empty hash inline (`--- {}\n`) rather than on its own line, so the old `.sub(/\A---\n/, "")` never matched and left the doc-start marker in the output; that stray `--- ` broke Cortex's own config parser ("cannot unmarshal !!str `--- {}`"), crash-looping the service under krikri only (py's cortex was genuinely `active`, HTTP 200 on `/ready`). Fixed with a regex that also matches the inline form. (2) The "reload cortex services" handler loops over `cortex_services \| dict2items`, empty in this role's all-in-one mode - real Ansible skips the whole handler ("All items skipped") and counts it in `skipped=`; `execute_handler_loop` fell through its own empty loop silently, and `record_handler_result`'s `already_displayed` branch counted the no-op result as `ok` instead (py `ok=24 skipped=13` vs cr `ok=25 skipped=12`, off by one each way). Fixed by returning a skipped result (with a "skipping:" line) when `loop_items.empty?`. (3) `plugins/systemd.cr`'s `active?` had the same `systemctl is-active`-exit-code bug as `service.cr`'s 0.9.648 fix (a separate plugin, separate check, so that fix didn't carry over) - a crash-looping `activating` unit read as not-running, reissuing `systemctl start` and reporting `changed=true` every rerun on "ensure cortex all-in-one service is started and enabled". Fixed identically (read raw `ActiveState`, treat `active`/`activating` as running). Two cosmetic-only spec-file coverage gaps also confirmed, not fixed: generic `PLAY [Play 1]`/`TASK [Task 1]` labels on a nameless play/task (real Ansible derives `PLAY [all]`/`TASK [role : action]`), and an extra "Host 'localhost' has no user specified" inventory warning krikri prints that real Ansible doesn't - both noted in KNOWN_MISSING.md. Times: cold cr 5.7s; warm py 12.4s vs cr 6.1s |
| cloudalchemy.ipmi_exporter | ubuntu | ✅ clean, identical (`ok=8 changed=5` cold, `ok=7 changed=0` warm both) - installs the exporter binary/service without actually probing BMC/IPMI hardware, so no real hardware dependency at deploy time. Times: cold py 6.0s vs cr 1.5s (4.0x); warm py 4.6s vs cr 0.7s (6.8x) |
| gantsign.kubernetes | ubuntu | ✅ clean, identical both cold and warm (`ok=6 changed=2 failed=1` cold, `ok=6 changed=0 failed=1` warm, both) - both fail at "Add kubernetes repo" (upstream `apt.kubernetes.io` legacy repo deprecated, environmental/role-side, not a krikri bug). Times: cold py 57.5s vs cr 18.1s (3.2x); warm py 49.4s vs cr 4.9s (10.1x) |
| gantsign.intellij-plugins | ubuntu | ✅ clean, identical both cold and warm (`ok=1 skipped=1` both - role's own default `users: []` skips its one `include_tasks` on both engines). Two cosmetic-only differences noted, not fixed (same class as cloudalchemy.cortex's above, see KNOWN_MISSING.md): generic `PLAY [Play 1]`/`TASK [Task 1]` labels, and an extra "no user specified" inventory warning. Times: cold py 2.86s vs cr 0.12s; warm py 2.0s vs cr 0.11s |

### Round 304: batch 2 of the 100-role campaign (1 of 4)

| Stouts.apt | ubuntu | ⚠️ real krikri-playbook bug, fixed in 0.9.650: `should_update_cache?` stat'd `/var/lib/apt/lists/partial` (a transient staging dir `apt-get update` barely touches) instead of real Ansible's own two-path check (`/var/lib/apt/periodic/update-success-stamp` if present, else the `/var/lib/apt/lists` directory's own mtime). The stale/old mtime made `age > cache_valid_time` almost always true, so krikri refreshed (and reported `changed: true` for) a cache real Ansible correctly saw as still valid via `apt_cache_valid_time: 3600` and left alone - cold `ok=3 changed=1 failed=1` (py) vs `ok=3 changed=2 failed=1` (cr); both fail identically on `python-apt`/`python-pycurl` (Python-2-era package names, gone on Ubuntu 22.04 - role-side, not a krikri bug). Fixed by checking the same two real paths; re-verified live - warm rerun now `ok=3 changed=0 failed=1` both, byte-identical. No dedicated spec (same live-mutation-only convention as `service.cr`/dpkg/crontab). Times: cold py 6.1s vs cr 21.6s (pre-fix, includes the spurious cache refresh); warm py 5.9s vs cr 1.3s (4.5x, post-fix) |
| Stouts.celery | ubuntu | ✅ identical both-fail: role's own `tasks/main.yml` uses the ansible-core-removed `include:` action, `rc=1` both |
| Stouts.denyhosts | ubuntu | ✅ identical both-fail: role's own dependency `Stouts.python` uses the removed `include:` action, `rc=1` both. One process note: `keepassxc-cli show` needs `-s`/`--show-protected` or the password field comes back as the literal string "PROTECTED" - now documented in `CLAUDE.local.md` |

Message-wording drift found (cosmetic only, not fixed): real ansible-core 2.17.14 now says `[DEPRECATED]: ansible.builtin.include has been removed...`, krikri's hardcoded text still matches the 2.19.4 wording it was originally verified against - documented in KNOWN_MISSING.md rather than chased, since the "correct" wording is a moving target across ansible-core versions |

### Round 308-311: batch 3 of the 100-role campaign - all 4 clean, no fixes needed

All 4 roles hit the same already-well-documented removed-`include:`/environmental-failure classes; no krikri divergence found in any of them.

| Stouts.elasticsearch | ubuntu | ✅ identical both-fail: role's own `tasks/main.yml` uses the removed `include:` action, `rc=1` both |
| Stouts.backup | ubuntu | ✅ identical both-fail: same removed `include:` action, `rc=1` both |
| Stouts.deploy | ubuntu | ✅ identical both-fail: same removed `include:` action (same message-wording drift as `Stouts.django` above, not re-documented), `rc=1` both |
| bertvv.bind | ubuntu | ✅ clean, identical both cold and warm. Both fail at "Main BIND config file" - the role's own template sets `dnssec-enable`, an option removed from BIND's `named.conf` grammar on Ubuntu 22.04's bind9 version; `named-checkconf` rejects it identically on both engines (role-side/environmental). Cold: `ok=9 changed=3 failed=1 skipped=12` both (py 47.96s / cr 34.8s). Warm: `ok=9 changed=0 failed=1 skipped=12` both (py 10.96s / cr 2.64s, 4.1x) - package-default bind9 config stays live under both, confirmed `active`/`enabled` identically |
| bertvv.dhcp | ubuntu | ⚠️ real divergence found, confirmed, NOT fixed (too invasive to do safely solo - see KNOWN_MISSING.md). The role's "Install config file" task uses `template:`'s `validate: 'dhcpd -t -cf %s'`. Real Ansible stages the rendered file in its own `remote_tmp` dir (`~/.ansible/tmp/ansible-tmp-.../`) before validating - `dhcpd`'s AppArmor confinement doesn't cover that path, so `dhcpd -t` gets "Permission denied" and the whole play fails there (`ok=11 changed=5 failed=1` cold, `ok=10 changed=0 failed=1` warm, identical both runs). `plugins/template.cr` instead stages its validate temp file inside `dest_dir` itself (`/etc/dhcp/.krikri-playbook-template-*.tmp`, a deliberate fix for a real cross-device-rename bug found on konstruktoid-hardening - see that code's own comment) - AppArmor allows reads from `/etc/dhcp/`, so validation passes and krikri proceeds where real Ansible can't (`ok=14 changed=8 failed=0` cold, `ok=12 changed=1 failed=0` warm). End state converges anyway: `isc-dhcp-server` ends up `failed` on BOTH hosts (py's because the config was never applied; cr's because the role's default config can't actually bind on this generic cloud VM's interfaces, a separate environmental limit `dhcpd -t`'s syntax-only check doesn't catch) |
| bertvv.mariadb | ubuntu | ⚠️ real krikri-playbook bug, fixed in 0.9.651: `template:`/`copy:` used to silently `Dir.mkdir_p` a missing single-file destination directory instead of failing like real Ansible does ("Destination directory X does not exist") - the divergence only ever showed up once a role's destination genuinely didn't exist yet, which the ~1250 other roles tested so far never happened to hit. Surfaced by the role's "Add official MariaDB repository (yum)" task templating into `/etc/yum.repos.d` on Ubuntu, where that directory never exists: py correctly refused (`ok=4 changed=0 failed=1`); cr silently created the directory and wrote the file (`ok=5 changed=1 failed=1`, diverging at a LATER task instead). Fixed in both `plugins/template.cr` and `plugins/copy.cr`'s single-file paths (the separate directory-recursive-copy path in `copy.cr` was left untouched - different, already-correct semantics). Re-verified live: cr now matches `ok=4 changed=0 failed=1` exactly. Regression specs added (`spec/integration/missing_dest_dir_spec.cr`). |

### Round 500-503: first batch run via a non-agentic script (`~/scratch/bench/`), not a per-role LLM agent

The 4-agents-in-parallel process from rounds 300-315 spent a large amount of tokens on work that's purely mechanical (provision, install, run twice, diff counts, teardown) - so this round switched to a plain bash pipeline (`run_role.sh`/`run_batch.sh`/`diff_summary.sh`, adapted from the pre-existing `roundNNN/run_role.sh` pattern used in rounds 150-200) that runs entirely without an LLM in the loop; a human/agent only reads the resulting diff table afterward. Two real script bugs were caught and fixed during this first run (not krikri-playbook bugs - bugs in the new harness itself): a status marker written right after Terraform's own colored output landed concatenated onto its non-newline-terminated last line, breaking a line-anchored `grep` for it and producing a false "did not finish cleanly" on every successful run; and `/usr/bin/time` appending a "Command exited with non-zero status" line into its own `-o` timing file on any non-zero exit, needing `-q` to suppress.

| bertvv.collectd | ubuntu | ✅ clean, identical both cold and warm (`ok=2 changed=0 failed=1` both - role-side/environmental, not investigated further) |
| bertvv.httpd | ubuntu | ✅ clean, identical both cold and warm (`ok=1 changed=0 failed=1` both - role-side/environmental) |
| singleplatform-eng.nginx | ubuntu | ⚠️ real divergence on cold, converged clean on warm - likely a package-install timing race, not investigated further given cost. Cold: py `ok=14 changed=7 failed=0` vs cr `ok=13 changed=6 failed=1` - cr's "reload nginx" handler failed ("nginx.service is not active, cannot reload") right after the "Copy nginx configuration in place" task, even though the earlier "Ensure nginx is enabled to start at boot" task saw it as already active on both engines. Warm rerun matched exactly (`ok=12 changed=0 failed=0` both) - nginx was active again by then. Given krikri's consistently much faster wall-clock execution (documented across nearly every role in this table), the likely cause is the same class already noted for `Oefenweb.influxdb`: krikri outruns nginx's own async post-install startup, which real Ansible's slower per-task overhead incidentally waits out. Not chased further with additional live-host cycles |
| singleplatform-eng.awscli | ubuntu | ✅ clean, identical both cold and warm (`ok=1 changed=0 failed=1` both - role-side/environmental) |

'Is a directory'") - root cause found after the earlier local isolated repro (which used `connection: local` and missed it entirely): `TaskExecutor#inline_copy_source_content` reads a small `src:` file's content on the controller and rewrites the task to `content:` instead, but ONLY for a non-local connection - exactly the case the local repro couldn't trigger. That conversion routes through `copy.cr`'s `handle_content_copy`, which never had the dest-is-directory basename-append logic `handle_file_copy`'s own `src:`-based path already has. Fixed by having `inline_copy_source_content` also set `__original_src_basename` (the same param the large-file staging path already relies on) and having the `content:` dispatch point append it to an existing-directory dest. Verified with a spec that fails without the fix and passes with it (confirmed by temporarily reverting and rebuilding). py `ok=7 changed=5 failed=0 skipped=4` vs cr (pre-fix) `ok=6 changed=4 failed=1 skipped=1`. Not re-verified live (round524 already ran before this fix landed) - flagging for live re-confirmation in a future round. Times: cold py 90.84s vs cr 88.12s; warm py 21.10s vs cr 0.57s |

| jdauphant.dns | ubuntu | ⚠️ Fixed in 0.9.658: `when: dns_forced_in_dhclientconf and item.value != ""` (`dns_forced_in_dhclientconf` defaults to a whole-value template, `"{{ansible_os_family == 'Debian' or ansible_os_family == 'Redhat'}}"`) failed with "Conditional result (True) was derived from value of type 'str'. Conditionals must have a boolean result." Root cause: real Ansible's Jinja2-native templating preserves the boolean TYPE through variable storage for a whole-value template like this; this codebase's string-based substitution renders the same semantic value as the literal text "True", indistinguishable at the strict-conditional-boolean check from a genuinely non-boolean string - reproduced locally as a plain BARE `when: dns_forced_in_dhclientconf` failing identically, not just the `and` form (the live round's "only the last item fails" shape was an artifact of that round's specific context, not the actual scope of the bug). Fixed with a carve-out in `conditional_evaluator.cr`'s strict check for a raw string of exactly "True"/"False" (still raises for any other non-boolean string). py `ok=4 changed=2 failed=0` vs cr (pre-fix) `ok=3 changed=1 failed=1`. Not re-verified live (round563 already ran before this fix landed). Times: cold py 11.81s vs cr 3.49s; warm py 15.94s vs cr 0.46s |

### Round 600-659: second 60-role campaign via the non-agentic bench script

7 real krikri-playbook bugs found and fixed in this batch (0.9.659-0.9.663), all found and fixed AFTER the batch completed (not live-reverified against fresh infra, per this session's cost-conscious approach - flagged for live re-confirmation in a future round): the dedicated `with_first_found:` keyword's `include_vars:` path never honored a custom `loop_control: loop_var:` name (13 `arillso.*` roles: docker, motd, ntp, openvpn, sshd, sudoers, defaultuser, fqdn, localization, logrotate, network, repositories, plus a related but distinct fix below for authorized_key); `with_first_found:`'s own `paths:` sub-key was silently discarded, always falling back to hardcoded search roots (arillso.authorized_key, a custom `paths: ['distribution']`); a checksum: url file holding nothing but a bare hex hash (no filename) was never recognized (githubixx.kubectl, dl.k8s.io's own `.sha512` files); `selectattr()`/`rejectattr()` didn't accept a dotted attribute path into a nested dict (githubixx.containerd's `selectattr('stat.exists', ...)`); and a registered loop's per-item results never exposed a custom `loop_control: loop_var:` name, only the literal `item` (same containerd task's `map(attribute='path')`, a second independent bug in the same investigation). 2 roles hit real Galaxy-404s (githubixx.consul-ca, githubixx.peervpn). arillso.chocolatey is a Windows-only role (real mismatch running on Linux, not chased). jriguera.configdrive's own role uses a removed/invalid `| success` Jinja filter - real Ansible ALSO fails on it, just earlier than krikri does; both engines fail on a genuinely broken role, not worth chasing. The `wtanaka.*`/`jriguera.*` (except configdrive)/`DavidWittman.*` clusters were all clean, mostly near-instant role-side-early-exit roles.

### Round 700-759: third 60-role campaign via the non-agentic bench script

This round also surfaced and fixed a serious, separate infrastructure issue while investigating why GitHub Actions CI had failed on every push since v0.9.646: missing CI system libraries, plus a 9-commit dependency drift where krikri's vendored `crinja` fork was pinned to a stale tag (`crystal-play-0.9.18`) missing several fixes this whole session had been silently testing against via a locally-drifted `lib/crinja` checkout. See `git log` (`a11b701`..`17af1e6`) for that investigation - not role-testing work, so not detailed further here.

4 real krikri-playbook bugs found and fixed in this batch (0.9.665-0.9.667): the `*` operator's string-repeat branch only accepted `Int32` for the numeric operand, not the `Int64` the `\| int` filter actually produces (jtyr.motd, fixed upstream in `crinja` itself, re-pinned as `crystal-play-0.9.20`); `and`/`or` conditional evaluation wrongly applied ansible-core 2.19's strict boolean-result requirement to each individual sub-operand instead of only the whole expression's final result, rejecting a perfectly valid non-boolean truthy operand mid-chain (ANXS.postgresql); and a relative `include_vars:` path in a role's own top-level `tasks/main.yml` (not reached via `include_tasks:`) had no matching search root at all for a role with no `vars/` dir (jnv.debian-backports). 1 more real gap found but NOT fixed - documented in KNOWN_MISSING.md as a major architectural undertaking, not a quick fix: real Ansible's private `_AnsibleLazyTemplateDict` internals preserve a dict variable built via `.update()` side-effect + concatenation as a genuine dict all the way through; this engine's plain string substitution can't replicate that (jtyr.motd's OWN remaining failure after its Int64 fix, and jtyr.nsswitch). 3 roles were Galaxy-404 (ANXS.mysql, jtyr.dracut, jtyr.environment). jnv.iojs-debian is a genuinely dead technology (io.js merged back into Node.js in 2015) - both engines fail on the same broken/gone upstream repo, at different points, not chased. The rest were clean, mostly the `jtyr.*` cluster's near-instant role-side-early-exit roles.

### Round 800-859: fourth 60-role campaign via the non-agentic bench script

10 real krikri-playbook bugs found and fixed in this batch (0.9.668-0.9.677), several of them whole-run crashes rather than recap-count mismatches: a role-local underscore-prefixed custom module (`_check_platform`) was wrongly treated as resolved instead of unavailable (amtega.check_platform, amtega.epel); a handler using `include_tasks:` crashed outright, never having been given the same handling as a regular task's own `include_tasks:` (Anthony25.unbound); `include_tasks:`/`import_tasks:` targeting a file outside the including file's own `tasks/` dir (ansible-network.cisco_ios, ansible-network.cisco_nxos) or a comment-only/blank file (ansistrano.deploy, ansistrano.rollback, cbrunnkvist.ansistrano-symfony-deploy, bmeme.drupal) both crashed instead of resolving/no-opping like real Ansible; a task-less play skipped fact-gathering entirely instead of still running it (arubanetworks.aoscx_role, arubanetworks.aos_wlan_role); `ansible_virtualization_role` and `ansible_interfaces` facts were missing outright, crashing a `when:` that referenced them (Ansible-Security-Compliance.rhel7-role-hipaa; brianshumate.consul); the strict `{% %}` block-tag scan wrongly flagged a Jinja2 global function call (`namespace(...)`) or its own kwarg names as undefined variables, and `lookup('template', ...)` leaked a `#jinja2:` directive line into its output and ignored `template_vars=` (bimdata.ferm, partially - see KNOWN_MISSING.md for the remaining Crinja-side gap); `get_url`'s `headers:` param only ever supported a comma-separated string, not the real dict role authors actually pass, sending a malformed header name and getting rejected outright by a real API (caddy_ansible.caddy_ansible); and a bracket-indexed variable whose own value was itself unrendered `{{ }}` text wasn't re-rendered before indexing into it (atosatto.docker-swarm). 6 roles were Galaxy-404 (ahuffman.aide, ahuffman.lvm, AlphaNodes.postgresql_client, AnsibleShipyard.ansible-zookeeper, avinetworks.aviconfig, avinetworks.avisdk). AerisCloud.disk and bobbyrenwick.pip are real upstream-role bugs interacting with ansible-core 2.19's own strict-conditional-boolean enforcement (a pre-existing documented gap, not new). 9 roles produced `rc=1` with no recap on both engines identically (a pre-recap failure both engines hit the same way, e.g. an unmet role dependency) - not chased individually since they're not divergences. `brunobenchimol.certbot_dns`, `alannix_lw.lacework_agent_ansible_role`, and `appsilon.mount_efs` are all now triaged - see their own rows below (fixed, fixed, and a real newly-found `template:` strict-undefined gap respectively).

| appsilon.mount_efs | ubuntu | ✅ Fixed (0.9.725) - "install | build amazon-efs-utils" (`command: .../build-deb.sh, args: {creates: ".../build/amazon-efs-utils*deb"}`) re-ran the build script on every single warm rerun instead of correctly no-opping. Two compounding bugs in `command:`'s own `creates:`/`removes:` handling, both live-verified against ansible-core 2.19.4: (1) real Ansible's own check is a GLOB (`glob.glob(path)`), not a literal path - `File.exists?` never matches a path containing `*` at all; (2) a creates:/removes:-guarded skip is an ORDINARY "ok" result (`changed: false`), never a task-level "skipping:" - this codebase's own `skipped: true` diverted it into the wrong recap bucket. Same two bugs, same fix, in `shell:`/`script:`/`expect:` (all four share the same idiom) - `unarchive:`'s own `creates:` is deliberately UNCHANGED (previously live-verified, round195, to be a genuine task-level skip in real Ansible - a different module, different real behavior). `spec/integration/command_spec.cr`, `spec/integration/shell_spec.cr`. Times: cold py 73.92s vs cr 68.78s; warm py 15.99s vs cr 2.83s |

### Round 900-999: 100-role campaign against roles never previously tested (agentic session, sourced live from the Galaxy API by download count, filtered against every role already in this file)

6 real krikri-playbook bugs found and fixed in this batch (0.9.703-0.9.708), triaged from 32 divergences out of 100 roles run: `ansible_verbosity` (the -v/-vv/-vvv magic var) didn't exist at all, and `debug: verbosity:`'s own gate was separately dead code even once added, because `debug:`/`assert:`/`fail:`/`set_fact:`/`pause:` run as controller-only action plugins that never reach the remote-dispatch path those params were being injected into (marcinpraczko.goss-install); `Hash#crinja_call` had no `.copy()` (Python dict.copy()), fixed in the vendored crinja fork itself, crystal-play-0.9.22 (ipr-cnrs.nftables); `with_first_found:`'s custom `paths:` sub-key only ever resolved a relative entry against the role root, when real Ansible (verified live against ansible-core 2.19.12) resolves it against the directory of the file the `with_first_found:` task is itself written in - hit identically by three independent roles (sbaerlocher.powercfg/.onedrive/.domain-membership); `from_yaml` raised on an already-structured (non-string) value instead of passing it through unchanged, matching real Ansible's own type guard (confirmed live: `from_json` DOES raise on the same input, `from_yaml` does not) (christiangda.awscli_configure); `with_subelements:`'s source list resolver only understood a bare/dotted variable reference, so a filter-chain source (`... | selectattr(...) | list`) resolved to nil instead of the real (possibly empty) list, running the task once with `item` unbound instead of correctly skipping an empty result (GROG.authorized-key); and a genuinely SYMBOLIC file mode (`u+x,g+x`, `a+x`) on `copy:`/`template:` was silently dropped (only all-digit octal modes were recognized), leaving a script non-executable for a later `command:` task to fail against - hit identically by two independent roles (grzegorznowak.nvm_node, srsp.oracle-java).

21 roles showed a real divergence not yet root-caused as of the initial batch (flagged in their own rows below rather than guessed at). 4 roles were Galaxy-404. 2 roles hit an infra-side issue this round (a provisioning timeout and a Terraform apply failure) rather than a real engine divergence. `arillso.ca_certificates` and `sbaerlocher.do-agent` were both root-caused to the same `with_first_found:` `paths:` bug fixed above and re-verified CLEAN (round1000/1001); `ipr-cnrs.nftables` was root-caused to a second dict-method gap (`.update()`, crystal-play-0.9.23) beyond the `.copy()` fix, fixed in 0.9.714.

**All 21 divergences from this batch are now triaged** (continued across 0.9.715-0.9.724, see their own rows for detail): `tigattack.mergerfs`, `diodonfrost.amazon_codedeploy`, `srsp.oracle-java` re-verified already-fixed by earlier 0.9.71x work; `nicolai86.prepare-release`, `inmotionhosting.wordpress`, `Graylog2.graylog`, `CyVerse-Ansible.ez`, `stackhpc.systemd_networkd`, `sscheib.openwrt_bootstrap`, `nvidia.enroot`, `ome.ice`, `dokku_bot.ansible_dokku` (partial), `entercloudsuite.mysql`, `GROG.reboot` real engine bugs found and fixed; `lenovo.lxca-inventory`, `newrelic.newrelic-infra`, `wcm_io_devops.conga_aem_dispatcher_flush`/`.conga_aem_packages`/`.aem_dispatcher`, `inverse_inc.gitlab_buildpkg_tools` root-caused to the documented custom/unavailable-module scope cut or a role-content quirk, not fixed; `Dell-Networking.dellos-vlt`/`.dellos-users` confirmed environmental (real Ansible can't even parse the playbook - missing `dellemc.os9` collection).

**Continued triage (0.9.715-0.9.717), working through the remaining divergences from local role caches + real-Ansible-in-Docker verification (no fresh hosts needed for these)**: `diodonfrost.amazon_codedeploy` and `tigattack.mergerfs` were both already-fixed-but-unverified (predate 0.9.712/0.9.71x fixes already shipped this round - re-confirmed locally, no new engine change). Three NEW real bugs found and fixed: **with_items:'s own implicit one-level flatten across multiple list-valued sources was never implemented** (`with_items: ["{{ list_a }}", "{{ list_b }}"]` bound each outer list as a single `item` instead of iterating every inner element - `loop:` correctly does NOT do this, only `with_items:` - found via nicolai86.prepare-release, fixed 0.9.715, `spec/integration/with_items_flatten_spec.cr`); **a `when:` type test (`is string`/`is number`/etc.) on a bare function-CALL expression** (`lookup('vars', item) is not string`) hit ConditionalEvaluator's hand-rolled type-test shortcut before the generic Crinja-delegation fallback ever ran, using the unparsed call text itself as a literal vars-hash key - always undefined, so "is not string" was unconditionally true (found via inmotionhosting.wordpress, whose entire massive role tree stopped after task 6 as a result: cr ok=5 vs py ok=120 - fixed 0.9.716, `spec/integration/when_is_test_on_function_call_spec.cr`); and **`when:` on a role entry (a `roles:` play-level entry OR a `meta/main.yml` dependency) was entirely unimplemented** - RoleLoader had no notion of it at all, silently dropping it (worse: leaking through as a fake role var literally named "when"), so a role/dependency's tasks ran unconditionally regardless of its own `when:` (found via Graylog2.graylog's meta dependency on lean_delivery.java, `when: graylog_install_java` undefined - real Ansible skips the whole dependency, krikri ran it and hit a real network 404 partway through - fixed 0.9.717, propagated onto every task the role expands to same as `import_role:`'s own when: propagation, `spec/integration/role_dependency_when_spec.cr`). `nicolai86.prepare-release` is partially fixed by the with_items flatten (directory iteration now correct) but still carries two unexplained/out-of-scope items: an undocumented `action: deployment_facts` module real Ansible reports "ok" for with no visible effect, and the role's own genuinely-undefined `deploy_to`/`user` vars (which this harness's bare invocation never provided) failing per-flattened-item on krikri vs. atomically on the whole templated loop source on real Ansible - a real, smaller remaining behavioral gap, not this role's headline bug.
