| entercloudsuite.mysql | ubuntu | ✅ Fixed (0.9.721) - two compounding bugs, both root-caused via a disposable Docker+sshd target (real remote SSH, not connection: local) rather than fresh Atlantic.net hosts. (1) `ansible_python_interpreter` was being set UNCONDITIONALLY during fact-gathering (0.9.652) - live-verified against ansible-core 2.19.4 that a real remote SSH target never defines this var at all (only a genuine `connection: local` target does, where it's just `sys.executable`) - so the role's own `{% if 'python3' in ansible_python_interpreter|default('') %}` (deciding the `python-mysqldb`/`python3-mysqldb` package name) always took the wrong, krikri-only-defined branch. Now threaded through as a real remote-vs-local distinction (`facts_gatherer.cr`, `spec/integration/facts_spec.cr`). (2) Fixing (1) exposed a SECOND, previously-masked bug: the `{% if %}` strict-undefined scan raised on the now-genuinely-undefined `ansible_python_interpreter` even though it's piped through `| default('')` - `default()` exists specifically to suppress Undefined, and this scan only ever carved out an identifier BEING a filter's own name (preceded by `\|`), never one immediately FOLLOWED by `\| default(...)` (`variable_substitutor.cr`, `spec/unit/variable_substitutor_blocktag_undefined_spec.cr`). | Frzk.chrony | ubuntu | ✅ Fixed (0.9.853) - `include_tasks: "{{ lookup('first_found', findme) }}"` (no `paths:`, candidate list containing `Debian.yml`) picked up the role's own `vars/Debian.yml` (a vars mapping, not a task list) and died "Included tasks file must be a YAML list", because the lookup form's no-`paths:` default search wrongly included `vars/`. Real Ansible's no-`paths:` search is the role ROOT, then role root/`tasks/`, then the play basedir - never vars//files//templates/ (probed live against 2.19.4). NOT the task-level `vars:`-leak round72000's triage suspected. Re-verified live: both engines now reach and fail at the same task (the role's own non-root `Permission denied` on `/etc/chrony`, identical locally). | f500.ufw | ubuntu | ✅ Fixed (0.9.855) - the warm rerun's full SSH lockout (`ok=0 failed=2`, `ssh: connect ... port 22: Connection timed out` on every task) after the cold run's `ufw default deny incoming` + `ufw --force enable` (no allow rules) was NOT a ufw-command divergence: the lockout hits real Ansible identically once its own master socket is moved away (verified live - the role genuinely locks the host against NEW incoming connections). The divergence was krikri's pid-scoped SSH control-socket directory (0.9.770): a new krikri process could never find the cold run's still-alive ControlPersist master and dialed fresh, while real Ansible's stable `~/.ansible/cp/` socket carries the warm rerun. Fixed with a stable control dir (`/tmp/.krikri-playbook-ssh/`); warm rerun now succeeds via master reuse (`ok=4 changed=0`, byte-identical to real Ansible's warm recap), concurrency stress (cross-host 5x + same-host pairs) clean. | GROG.reboot | ubuntu | ✅ Fixed (0.9.722) - `wait_for_connection:` (the task right after `shell: shutdown -r now`) ran the same way every other module does: uploaded to and executed ON the target, over the exact connection it exists to wait for. The instant a real reboot took the connection down, the upload itself failed ("Plugin execution failed on remote") instead of patiently retrying, defeating the module's whole purpose. Made controller-only (matching `fetch:`'s existing pattern) with a real retry-until-connect loop via `BasePlugin#remote_exec`; also excluded from the play's own up-front batch-upload pass, which was ALSO eagerly trying to upload it before any task ran at all. Live-verified end to end against a disposable Docker+sshd target (not a real reboot) with sshd down at task start: brought back up mid-wait succeeds (picked up on the next retry, ~10s total against an 8s downtime), and never brought back up correctly times out at the configured `timeout:` (~5s, bounded, not hanging). `spec/integration/wait_for_connection_spec.cr`. | nvidia.enroot | ubuntu | ✅ Fixed (0.9.718) - two bugs, both now resolved. Original crash (v0.9.702, `package_facts: manager: apt` unrecognized) fixed 0.9.709. Second, deeper bug found on real-host re-confirm (round1002, then reproduced again on a dedicated round1010 re-run against 0.9.717): `remove existing enroot package if we are changing version`'s `when: ansible_facts.packages['enroot'][0]['version'] != enroot_version_string` ran on every warm rerun (removing+reinstalling both packages every time) because `enroot_version_string: "{{ enroot_version }}-{{ enroot_release }}"` is a MULTI-span templated value (two separate `{{ }}` spans with a literal "-" between them, not one whole-string span) - `rerender_if_templated`'s single-span fast path (4 independent copies: ConditionalEvaluator, ComparisonEvaluator, FilterEngine, VariableLookup) checked only "starts with {{ and ends with }}", which is ALSO true for a multi-span string, then naively sliced off just the first/last 2 characters, leaving the literal "-{{"/"}}-" text in the "expression" - unparseable, so the comparison never saw the real rendered value. Root-caused via a disposable Docker+sshd target (not Atlantic.net) running the exact real `nvidia/enroot` `.deb`, confirmed fixed the same way after the fix, then the underlying Crystal bug (`String#count("{{")` counts CHARACTER-set membership, not substring occurrences - degenerately "correct" for single-char args, silently wrong for a 2-char substring) is what made the FIRST attempt at this fix not work at all until caught by the full spec suite. All 4 copies fixed; regression spec in `spec/unit/conditional_evaluator_spec.cr`. | sscheib.openwrt_bootstrap | ubuntu | ✅ Fixed (0.9.724) - `vars/main.yml` defines its own `_bts_install_full_python`/`_bts_update_opkg_cache` as whole-value templates indirecting through `default(...)` to a REAL Python bool default (`_def_bts_install_full_python: false`). Every `rerender_if_templated`-shaped helper across both evaluators (re-rendering a variable whose own raw value is itself unrendered Jinja, to recover its real type) did `(JSON.parse(rendered) rescue nil) || JSON::Any.new(rendered)` - for a real bool this ALWAYS falls to the rescue branch (`JSON.parse("False")` raises; JSON requires lowercase `false`), wrapping the STRING "False" instead of the real boolean - so the role's own "Ensure variables are defined properly" assert (`_bts_install_full_python is boolean`) always failed regardless of the real (correct) value, stopping the role before its first real task ever ran. Fixed via one new shared `Krikri.parse_json_or_python_literal` helper (recognizes Python's own `True`/`False`/`None` spellings, and a single-quoted dict/list repr) used everywhere that pattern appeared. `spec/unit/conditional_evaluator_spec.cr`. Times: cold py 4.44s vs cr 4.18s; warm py 3.75s vs cr 0.36s |

| stackhpc.systemd_networkd | ubuntu | ✅ Fixed (0.9.720) - `when: systemd_networkd_network or systemd_networkd_link or systemd_networkd_netdev` (three empty-dict defaults, all falsy). ConditionalEvaluator's `or`/`and` splitting always coerced the whole chain to a plain Bool via `parts.any?`/`parts.all?`, never replicating real Python's own or/and semantics (return one specific operand's RAW value unchanged, never coerced) - so a chain of all-falsy non-boolean operands (real Python's `or` returns the LAST one, a dict here) silently evaluated `false` and kept running the gated task, instead of ansible-core 2.19's real "Conditionals must have a boolean result" rejection. Fixed by finding the real short-circuit-deciding operand (non-strict pass, matching real short-circuiting) and re-checking ONLY that one under strict - preserves the existing ANXS.postgresql fix (an `and` chain that short-circuits on a later real bool, ignoring earlier non-boolean-but-truthy strings) while now also catching this converse case. `spec/unit/conditional_evaluator_spec.cr`. Times: cold py 4.44s vs cr 2.81s; warm py 2.81s vs cr 0.39s |

## Round 60300-60499 (RHEL-family 200-role batch via krikri-role-tester, 0.9.795 -> 0.9.797)

200 previously-untested Galaxy roles (picked from Galaxy's live API, ordered by
download count, cross-checked against every role name already in this file),
run against RHEL family (Rocky 9.6) via `krikri-role-tester` across both the
Kata (154 roles) and Atlantic.net (46 roles) backends concurrently, cold+warm
on both engines. Result: 146 CLEAN, 46 DIVERGENT, 8 GALAXY_MISSING (404 on
Galaxy). Two real engine bugs found, fixed, and live-reverified on a fresh
Rocky 9.6 Kata host (0.9.796, 0.9.797); full `crystal spec` clean except the
one pre-existing Docker-daemon-required integration failure (unrelated).
Triage of the remaining divergences found several already-documented
scope-cut/open-gap classes recurring (role-private custom modules/filter
plugins, `local_action:`, out-of-scope Windows/SUSE modules) and a handful of
genuinely new open leads (a `length` filter / `None` mismatch, a Crinja
template include-path gap, an indirect-variable-lookup gap) flagged below for
a future round rather than fixed here.

| Role | OS | Status |
|---|---|---|

| inmotionhosting.monit | rocky | ✅ **FIXED 0.9.836** - root-caused: the role's own "Remove nonexistent services" task uses `item.var_name not in vars` (real Ansible's `vars` magic dict) to filter out any service whose backing variable (`apache_daemon`, etc.) was never defined by the playbook; `ConditionalEvaluator#evaluate_in`'s Hash-valued container (no Hash case in `#evaluate_value`'s own return union) fell through to a stringified compact-JSON dump and did a raw SUBSTRING search instead of a real key lookup, spuriously matching because `monitored_services` (itself one of the vars) holds the literal string "apache_daemon" nested inside it - inverting `not in vars` to false and leaving every service in the list. The later "Install service configs" loop then failed all 5 templates with `'apache_daemon' is undefined` instead of running zero iterations. Fixed in `ConditionalEvaluator#evaluate_in` (real key-membership test for a Hash container). Regression spec added (`spec/unit/conditional_evaluator_spec.cr`); verified live via an extracted repro matching the role's exact task shape. Times: cold py 51.43s vs cr 39.04s; warm py 12.08s vs cr 1.67s. |

Fixed in `ExpressionEvaluator`'s ternary branches. Not yet re-run end to end (large STIG role) - the specific failing task is confirmed fixed via an extracted repro matching the role's exact shell/regex. ## Round 71000-71700 (200-role Galaxy top-download batch + confirm reruns, all on fresh Atlantic.net Ubuntu-22.04 host pairs, 0.9.836 → 0.9.844)

First batch since Atlantic.net's server-limit increase (10 → 25). 200 never-before-tested roles drawn from Galaxy's top-download list, deduped against every role already in this table. All 4 Kata pairs hit a new, undiagnosed `cgroup.procs ... Device or resource busy` boot failure this round (44 roles lost, no automatic cross-backend retry in `krikri-role-tester`) - not an engine bug; those 44 were successfully requeued Atlantic-only and all completed. Given the expanded Atlantic capacity, Kata is no longer the default for these batches. 8 real engine bugs found and fixed (see `KNOWN_MISSING.md`'s round-71000 entry for the full narrative); every fix below was confirmed via a live full-role rerun on a fresh Atlantic host, not just a unit spec. Final counts: 147 clean/equivalent, 41 untestable (Galaxy 404), 12 genuinely divergent (scope cuts, harness gaps, or new open gaps - see notes).
